. The calculation first appeared in
[
25
]
, based on the same methods used for the kink
[
26
]
. We explain the subtleties by first recovering the
1+1
d result
m_{\rm soliton}=\frac{8m}{\beta^{2}}-\frac{m}{\pi}+O(\beta^{2})
17
17

17

\beta
is dimensionless in
1+1
d.
, and then we will continue to the
2+1
d case. In
1+1
d the mass counterterm takes the form
\delta m_{2,1d}^{2}=-\frac{m^{2}\beta^{2}}{8\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)
.

The naive expression for the
1+1
d energy correction is given by the zero-point energy, with the two counterterms:

E_{{\rm gs},1d}=\frac{8m}{\beta^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot 0+\frac{1}{2}\int dp\left(\frac{L_{x}}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{d\delta(p)}{dp}\right)\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}}+\frac{4}{m\beta^{2}}\delta m_{2,1d}^{2}+L_{x}\delta\Lambda_{0,1d}+O(\beta^{2}).

(5.12)

The vacuum energy should be subtracted from this equation:

\displaystyle E_{{\rm vac},1d}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int dp\frac{L_{x}}{2\pi}\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}}+L_{x}\delta\Lambda_{0,1d}+O(\beta^{2}),

(5.13)

\displaystyle m_{\rm soliton}

\displaystyle=E_{{\rm gs},1d}-E_{{\rm vac},1d}\equiv\frac{8m}{\beta^{2}}+\delta m_{\rm soliton},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\delta m_{\rm soliton}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{d\delta(p)}{dp}\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}}+\frac{4}{m\beta^{2}}\delta m_{2,1d}^{2}.

(5.15)

However, this method does not yield the right result, nor does it eliminate all of the divergences appearing in
2+1
d. In
[
26
]
, they used a finite but very large
L_{x}
, such that the modes are discrete. Since
L_{x}
is very large, the modes are approximately the same as in the continuum, with the phase shifts (
3.9
),(
3.11
) dictating the allowed momenta
{p^{\prime}}_{n}
by (
3.13
). Then, the integrals turn into discrete sums:

\delta m_{\rm soliton}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}^{\infty}\left(\sqrt{{p^{\prime}}_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}-\sqrt{p_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}(0-m)+\frac{4}{m\beta^{2}}\delta m_{2,1d}^{2},

(5.16)

where
p_{n}=\frac{2\pi n}{L_{x}}
are the allowed momenta in the vacuum. For all
n\neq 0
, the momenta in the soliton sector are related to the momenta in the vacuum sector by
{p^{\prime}}_{n}\approx p_{n}-\frac{\delta(p_{n})}{L_{x}}
. There is no
p=0
eigenvalue in the soliton sector; the bulk scalar has no standing wave. The massive
p=0
eigenvalue in the vacuum is mapped into the massless NGB in the presence of the soliton. Hence, the
n=0
state is the bulk scalar at rest in the vacuum with energy
m
and the NGB in the one-soliton sector with zero energy, and we treat it separately. For any function
f
:

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(f({p^{\prime}}_{n})-f(p_{n}))\approx\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{df(p)}{dp}\Bigg|_{p=p_{n}}\left(-\frac{\delta(p_{n})}{L_{x}}\right)\to-\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{df(p)}{dp}\delta(p),

(5.17)

where we change to a simple integral since the density of states is constant in the vacuum. By integration by parts, this relation can be modified:

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(f({p^{\prime}}_{n})-f(p_{n}))\to\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{d\delta(p)}{dp}f(p)+\frac{1}{2\pi}f(p)\delta(p)|^{0}_{\infty}.

(5.18)

A couple of things to note:

1.

The derivative
\frac{d\delta(p)}{dp}
is not sensitive to the discontinuity of
\delta(p)
at
p=0
. However, here, we need the boundary value of
\delta(p)
. Therefore, we considered half of the sum. A similar formula holds for the sum from
-\infty
to
-1
.

2.

The summation is from
n=1
and does not include
n=0
.

3.

(
5.18
) is almost the same as the naive subtraction in (
5.15
), the only difference is the boundary terms.

Using (
5.18
) for the sum inside (
5.16
) gives:

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\left(\sqrt{{p^{\prime}}_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}-\sqrt{p_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}\right)&\to\frac{1}{2}\left(2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(\frac{d\delta(p)}{dp}\sqrt{p_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}\right)+2\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{p_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}\delta(p)|^{0}_{\infty}\right)\\
&\to\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)-\frac{m}{\pi}+\frac{m}{2},\end{split}

(5.19)

where we implemented the sharp cutoff. Putting all parts together we obtain:

\delta m_{\rm soliton}=\left(\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)-\frac{m}{\pi}+\frac{m}{2}\right)-\frac{m}{2}+\frac{4}{m\beta^{2}}\left(-\frac{m^{2}\beta^{2}}{8\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)\right)=-\frac{m}{\pi},

(5.20)

which is the desired result.

Now, we move to
2+1
d. The analog of (
5.16
) is:

\begin{split}\delta E_{\rm string}=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}^{\infty}\left(\sqrt{q_{l}^{2}+{p^{\prime}}_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}-\sqrt{q_{l}^{2}+p_{n}^{2}+m^{2}}\right)\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}\left(|q_{l}|-\sqrt{q_{l}^{2}+m^{2}}\right)+\frac{4}{m\beta^{2}}\delta m_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.21)

Repeating the same steps as before for each
y
-momentum
q_{l}=\frac{2\pi l}{L_{y}}
yields:

\begin{split}\delta E_{\rm string}=\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-s|q_{l}|}\Bigg(&\frac{q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m^{2}-q_{l}^{2}}{2mq_{l}}\right)-q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m}{q_{l}}\right)}{\pi}+\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)\\
&+\frac{\pi\sqrt{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}-2m}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\left(|q_{l}|-\sqrt{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}\right)\Bigg)+\frac{4}{m\beta^{2}}\delta m_{2}^{2}L_{y},\end{split}

(5.22)

where we implemented the
y
-momentum regulator. The first two terms in the sum come from the integral, the third term from the boundary terms, and the fourth term is the
n=0
contribution. The last term is the counterterm contribution. Using the Euler-Maclaurin formula, it can be seen that the counterterm is canceled by the integral of the
\log
, and the string tension correction and the Casimir energy of the massless NGB are obtained. These are the perturbative terms in
mL_{y}
, and indeed, the Euler-Maclaurin series is a series in
mL_{y}
. To obtain the non-perturbative part one needs to evaluate the sums exactly. This is done in appendix
G
. The final result is:

\delta E_{\rm string}=-\frac{m^{2}}{4\pi}L_{y}-\frac{\pi}{6L_{y}}+\frac{1}{\pi L_{y}}\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right),

(5.23)

as in (
5.7
).

6
Scattering

In this section, the simplest scattering amplitudes along the string are calculated, those of two NGBs (a single NGB cannot decay by momentum conservation). For simplicity we discuss the case of an infinite string, avoiding the complications of recoil of the string when BPs are emitted.

The calculation can be done in each one of the formalisms used so far. However, while the BP interactions appear the same in both formalisms, the NGB interaction vertices are fewer in the stringy formalism. There are only three of them, rather than an infinite number in the direct formalism. Moreover, they lead to simpler integrals. Hence, we will use the stringy formalism. In appendix
E
, we calculate the two simple processes of 2NGB
\to
1BP and 2NGB
\to
2NGB in the direct formalism and verify that the results are the same as presented here.

The three vertices in the stringy formalism correspond to three processes that can happen at tree level, involving NGBs and BPs:

1.

Two NGBs to one bulk-photon.

2.

Two NGBs to two bulk-photons.

3.

One NGB to two bulk-photons. This process is prohibited by kinematics since the BP is massive and the NGBs are massless.

The second process is suppressed with respect to the first one by
\beta
. Still, it is kinematically favored, because the measure of the outgoing particles’ phase space is non-zero, unlike the first process. Two additional processes of order
\beta^{2}
are:

1.

Two NGBs to two NGBs.

2.

Two NGBs to one NGB and one BP.

To be careful with this theory, which violates
x
-momentum conservation and involves fields “living” in different dimensions, the scattering amplitudes will be derived from the correlators (with the canonical variable
X_{0}
) using the LSZ reduction formula. In the correlation function, we use general offshell momenta, and for the residue at the poles, onshell momenta are inserted. For the process
\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
into some out state,
\{-k_{1},-k_{2}\}
are used in the correlator. In this section, we will use the center of mass frame. Putting the momenta of the two NGBs onshell gives
k_{1}^{2}=k_{2}^{2}=0,\ k_{1}\cdot k_{2}=\frac{s}{2}
where, as usual,
s\equiv(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}
is the total energy squared. The other two Mandelstam variables are also used with two-momentum only:
t\equiv(k_{1}-k_{3})^{2},\ u\equiv(k_{1}-k_{4})^{2}
. We will denote the spatial part of
k
as usual by
\vec{k}
, although it has only one component.

Reducing the usual derivation of the total cross section from the scattering amplitude from
3+1
d to
1+1
d yields the same formula (see, e.g.,
[
27
]
pp.102-106, and the final result (4.79)). In
1+1
d, the total cross section is just a probability. The integration over the final state is different for a NGB, with
\frac{d\vec{k}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{2|\vec{k}|}
, and a BP, with
\frac{d\vec{k}dp}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\vec{k}^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}}
. The prefactor
\frac{1}{2E_{1}2E_{2}}\frac{1}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|}
equals
\frac{1}{2s}
for scattering of two NGBs.

In the following, we draw the Feynman diagrams for the momentum-space correlation functions calculated. Dashed lines denote NGBs, while solid lines are BPs. The momentum direction depends on a convention; we choose the following convention:

\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}

6.1
2 NGB to 1 BP

The three-point function
\langle X_{0}(-k_{1})X_{0}(-k_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,k_{3})\rangle
gets a tree-level contribution only from one diagram with one insertion of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
:

\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.5pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.95 L 0 65.98}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}}\\
&=\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(-k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3})\frac{i\pi\beta}{4m}(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})(m+ip){\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right).\end{split}

(6.1)

Putting the momenta onshell and implementing the scattering kinematics yield
(k_{3},p)=(\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}},0,p)
with
p^{2}+m^{2}=s
. Then:

i\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to(p,\vec{k}_{3}))=\frac{i\pi\beta s}{8m}(m\pm i\sqrt{s-m^{2}}){\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}\right),

(6.2)

depending on the sign of
p
. The total cross section is given by:

\begin{split}\sigma_{\rm 1BP}&=\frac{1}{2s}\int\frac{d\vec{k}_{3}dp}{(2\pi)^{2}2\sqrt{\vec{k}_{3}^{2}+m^{2}+p^{2}}}\left|\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to(p,\vec{k}_{3}))\right|^{2}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(\vec{k}_{3})\delta\left(\sqrt{\vec{k}_{3}^{2}+m^{2}+p^{2}}-\sqrt{s}\right)\\
&=\frac{\pi^{2}\beta^{2}s^{2}}{128m^{2}\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}\sech\left(\frac{\pi\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}\right)^{2}.\end{split}

(6.3)

6.2
2 NGB to 2 NGB

The scattering amplitude of two NGBs to two NGBs can be deduced from the four-point function
\langle X_{0}(-k_{1})X_{0}(-k_{2})X_{0}(k_{3})X_{0}(k_{4})\rangle
. It gets contributions from diagrams with two
\mathcal{V}_{1}
vertices, and splits into
s,t,u
channels:

\begin{split}&\langle X_{0}(-k_{1})X_{0}(-k_{2})X_{0}(k_{3})X_{0}(k_{4})\rangle_{\rm tree}=\\

{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{}}{}{{}}
{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.32pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 88.45 5.85 L 114.23 31.62}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.7071}{0.7071}{-0.7071}{0.7071}{81.40645pt}{21.70567pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.7071 0.7071 -0.7071 0.7071 112.64 30.03)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 L 0.71 1.99 L 2.35 0 L 0.71 -1.99 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
}}{
}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{77.69576pt}{4.3847pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 107.51 6.07)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{}
\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray={3.0pt,3.0pt},stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.5pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 72.95 2.09 L 121.1 50.23}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}}\\
&=\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{4}^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\left(-\frac{i\pi^{2}\beta^{2}}{16m^{2}}\right)(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}}{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}{\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)^{2}+(k_{1}\leftrightarrow-k_{3})+(k_{2}\leftrightarrow-k_{3}),\end{split}

(6.4)

where the
t
-channel is related to the
s
-channel by
-k_{2}\leftrightarrow k_{3}
, and the
u
-channel by
-k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{3}
. The integral is evaluated in appendix
G
:

I_{\rm 2NGB}(s,m)\equiv\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}}{s-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}{\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)^{2}=-\frac{m}{\pi^{2}}\left(2+\frac{s}{m\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}\psi_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{2m}\right)\right),

(6.5)

where
\psi_{1}
is the Polygamma function. The square root
\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}
takes the negative imaginary root for
s>m^{2}
(see appendix
G
). Using onshell momenta gives

k_{1}\cdot k_{2}=k_{3}\cdot k_{4}=\frac{s}{2}
. Without loss of generality we can take
t=0
and
u=-s
, and the final result is:

\begin{split}i\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},&\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{3},\vec{k}_{4})=i\beta^{2}\left(\frac{s^{2}}{16m}+\frac{s^{3}}{64m^{2}}\frac{\psi_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{2m}\right)}{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}-\frac{s^{3}}{64m^{2}}\frac{\psi_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{s+m^{2}}}{2m}\right)}{\sqrt{s+m^{2}}}\right).\end{split}

(6.6)

\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{3},\vec{k}_{4})
has an imaginary part for
s>m^{2}
as expected. This comes from the creation of a BP, and thus should be related to the amplitude of 2NGB
\to
1BP by the optical theorem. The integral for the optical theorem and the result are given in (
6.3
) (up to the
1/(2s)
factor). Note that unlike the standard situation in QFTs where there are integrals over
d
-dimensional momenta and a
d+1
-dimensional
\delta
-function, here the
x
-momentum does not have a
\delta
-function conservation. This also enables the appearance of a branch-cut in
2\to 2
scattering. The imaginary part of (
6.6
) comes from the
s
-channel solely for
s>m^{2}
:

\begin{split}2\imaginary(\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}))&=2\imaginary\left(\frac{\beta^{2}s^{3}}{64m^{2}}\frac{\psi_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}-i\frac{\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}\right)}{-i\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}\right)\\
&=\frac{\beta^{2}s^{3}}{64m^{2}\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}2\real\left(\psi_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}-i\frac{\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.7)

For evaluating the real part of
\psi_{1}
we use the Polygamma reflection formula:

\psi_{n}(z)-(-1)^{n}\psi_{n}(1-z)=-\pi\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}\cot(\pi z)

(6.8)

with
n=1
and
z=\frac{1}{2}-i\frac{\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}
, which gives:

2\real\left(\psi_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}-i\frac{\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}\right)\right)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{\pi\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}\right)}.

(6.9)

Inserting (
6.9
) into (
6.7
) reproduces (
6.3
), up to a factor
2s
present in the total cross section and not in the optical theorem.

Another point that should be addressed is the comparison to EST. Among all of the processes considered in this section, this is the only one existing in EST, as it involves only NGBs. Therefore, it can be compared to the EST result and should agree with it for
\sqrt{s}\ll m
. Indeed, the scattering amplitude for 2NGB
\to
2NGB is (see (24) in
[
2
]
, where
i=j=k=l
and
t+u=-s
):

\mathcal{M}^{\rm EST}_{\rm tree}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{3},\vec{k}_{4})=\frac{1}{2}\ell_{s}^{2}s^{2},

(6.10)

which is the leading term of (
6.6
), with
\frac{\beta^{2}}{16m}=\frac{1}{2T_{\rm cl}}=\frac{\ell_{s}^{2}}{2}
. The other terms are suppressed by the factor
\frac{\sqrt{s}}{m}
for
\sqrt{s}\ll m
, as expected.

The total cross section is given by:

\begin{split}\sigma_{\rm 2GB}&=\frac{1}{2s}\int\frac{d\vec{k}_{3}}{2\pi(2|\vec{k}_{3}|)}\frac{d\vec{k}_{4}}{2\pi(2|\vec{k}_{4}|)}\left|\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{3},\vec{k}_{4})\right|^{2}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4})\delta\left(|\vec{k}_{3}|+|\vec{k}_{4}|-\sqrt{s}\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{4s^{2}}\left|\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2})\right|^{2}.\end{split}

(6.11)

6.3
2 NGB to 1 NGB and 1 BP

The scattering amplitude of two NGBs to one NGB and one BP can be computed from the correlator
\langle X_{0}(-k_{1})X_{0}(-k_{2})X_{0}(k_{3})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,k_{4})\rangle
. The leading contribution comes from the insertion of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
\mathcal{V}_{3}
vertices in three different channels; each diagram splits to
x
-momentum conserving / violating parts due to
\mathcal{V}_{3A},\mathcal{V}_{3B}
. The correlation function is:

\begin{split}&\langle X_{0}(-k_{1})X_{0}(-k_{2})X_{0}(k_{3})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,k_{4})\rangle_{\rm tree}=\\
&\vbox{\hbox{
\hbox to105.91pt{\vbox to137.55pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 62.73895pt\lower-43.16669pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}
\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-39.83368pt}{-39.83368pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -55.12 -55.12)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.32pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 88.45 5.85 L 114.23 31.62}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.7071}{0.7071}{-0.7071}{0.7071}{81.40645pt}{21.70567pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.7071 0.7071 -0.7071 0.7071 112.64 30.03)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 L 0.71 1.99 L 2.35 0 L 0.71 -1.99 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
}}{
}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{77.69576pt}{4.3847pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 107.51 6.07)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{}
\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray={3.0pt,3.0pt},stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.5pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 72.95 2.09 L 121.1 50.23}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}}\\
&=\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{4}^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(-k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot\\
&\quad\Bigg(\frac{i\pi\beta^{2}}{(8m)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})(k_{3}^{2}+2(k_{3}\cdot k_{4}))p(m+ip){\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\frac{i}{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad-\frac{\pi^{2}\beta^{2}}{2(8m)^{\frac{3}{2}}}(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})\frac{k_{3}^{2}+2(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})}{ip-m}\int\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}{\csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p+p^{\prime})}{2m}\right){\sech}\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)\frac{p^{\prime 2}-p^{2}}{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}-p^{\prime 2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\Bigg)\\
+&(k_{1}\leftrightarrow-k_{3})+(k_{2}\leftrightarrow-k_{3}),\end{split}

(6.12)

where the second row comes from
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
and the third row from
\mathcal{V}_{3B}
. The integral is evaluated in appendix
G
:

\begin{split}I_{\rm 1NGB1BP}(s,m,p)\equiv&\int\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}{\csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p+p^{\prime})}{2m}\right){\sech}\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)\frac{p^{\prime 2}-p^{2}}{s-p^{\prime 2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}=\\
&\frac{-2p\left(\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}-m\right)+i\left(-s+m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\left(H_{-\frac{ip}{2m}+\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{2m}}-H_{\frac{ip}{2m}+\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{2m}}\right)}{2\pi\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}},\end{split}

(6.13)

where
H_{n}
is the (analytic continuation of the) harmonic number
n
. We put the momenta onshell, and use the kinematics
k_{1}\cdot k_{2}=\frac{s}{2}
,
k_{3}\cdot k_{4}=\frac{s-p^{2}-m^{2}}{2}
, and similar expressions for the other multiplications. Energy-Momentum conservation gives
|\vec{k}_{3}|=|\vec{k}_{4}|=\frac{s-p^{2}-m^{2}}{2\sqrt{s}}
and
\sqrt{\vec{k}_{4}^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}=\frac{s+p^{2}+m^{2}}{2\sqrt{s}}
, while
t=p^{2}+m^{2}-s
or
0
and
u=p^{2}+m^{2}-s-t
.
18
18

18

The value of
t
is easy to see using
(k_{1}-k_{3})^{2}
, since both are lightlike. However, it is also equal to
(k_{2}-k_{4})^{2}
as can be easily checked, although
k_{4}
is timelike.

The scattering amplitude is given by:

\begin{split}i\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{3},(p,\vec{k}_{4}))=&-\frac{\pi\beta^{2}(m+ip)}{64\sqrt{2}(m)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Bigg(2p\left(m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\\
&+\pi s\left(1-\frac{s}{m^{2}+p^{2}}\right)\left(I_{\rm 1NGB1BP}\left(m^{2}+p^{2}-s,m,p\right)+I_{\rm 1NGB1BP}(s,m,p)\right)\Bigg).\end{split}

(6.14)

The total cross section is given by:

\begin{split}\sigma_{\rm 1NGB+1BP}=&\frac{1}{2s}\int\frac{d\vec{k}_{3}}{2\pi(2|\vec{k}_{3}|)}\frac{d\vec{k}_{4}dp}{2\pi\left(2\sqrt{\vec{k}_{4}^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}\right)}\cdot\\
&\left|\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{3},(p,\vec{k}_{4}))\right|^{2}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4})\delta\left(|\vec{k}_{3}|+\sqrt{\vec{k}_{4}^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}-\sqrt{s}\right)\\
=&\frac{1}{2\pi s}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}dp\frac{\left|\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{k}_{3},(p,\vec{k}_{4}))\right|^{2}}{s-p^{2}-m^{2}}.\end{split}

(6.15)

This integral cannot be calculated analytically; but in any case such a computation does not make sense because the integral suffers from an IR divergence, when the
x
-momentum of the BP
p
is close to
\sqrt{s-m^{2}}
, and the momentum
k_{3}
of the NGB approaches zero. Naively one does not expect such a divergence to appear, since the NGB interactions come with positive momentum powers. These powers should cancel the measure factor
\frac{d\vec{k}_{3}}{2|\vec{k}_{3}|}
. However, the BP internal propagator in the
s
-channel of the insertion of the momentum-conserving part
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
becomes onshell when
k_{3}\to 0
, which makes
\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to\vec{0},(p,\vec{0}))
finite and
\sigma_{\rm 1NGB+1BP}
divergent.

Usually, an IR divergence in theories of massless particles such as massless QED indicates that the external states were not identified correctly, and that external states of massive particles need to be dressed with ‘clouds’ of soft massless particles. In our case, we believe that this is not the correct interpretation. One reason is that there are no IR divergences in one-loop diagrams involving the same process without the external soft NGB, that can cancel the divergence in the tree-level cross section. Another reason is that the one-loop correction to the BP propagator does not suffer from an IR divergence, so there is no indication that the propagation of this state needs to be corrected.

Instead, we believe that the correct interpretation is that our formalism does not correctly take into account the recoil of the string when it absorbs or emits particles carrying transverse momentum. Such a recoil is guaranteed by momentum conservation; naively one would think that it can be ignored for an infinite string (which can absorb finite momentum while remaining at zero velocity) but it seems that this is not the case. Since the recoil involves turning on low-momentum NGBs, it apparently leads to an IR divergence when we expand around the wrong final state which does not take this into account.

As a consistency check on our interpretation, we can show that IR divergences of this type appear only when the total transverse momentum of the emitted BPs is non-zero, such that the string indeed recoils. Consider a general diagram in which some number of bulk particles are emitted, and they in turn emit (from their external lines) NGBs. In the corresponding cross section computation, we have, as in (
6.15
), an integral
\frac{d\vec{k}_{i}}{2|\vec{k}_{i}|}
over the momentum of each such NGB. By itself this would give an IR divergence. The coupling in the
\mathcal{V}_{3}
vertex contains an extra power of
k_{i}
that cancels this divergence. However, we can still get a divergence, if in the
k_{i}\to 0
limit the extra internal BP propagator attached to this vertex becomes onshell. Since the external state is onshell, this happens only when the momentum
p
of this internal propagator is the same as that of the external particle, namely, it arises (as in the computation above) when we use the
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
vertex and not the
\mathcal{V}_{3B}
vertex. Using the form of the
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
vertex, one can show that for each NGB that is emitted from a leg corresponding to a BP with transverse momentum
p_{j}
, the logarithmic IR divergence in the
\vec{k_{i}}\to 0
limit is proportional to
p_{j}
(with a coefficient that does not depend on which external leg the NGB comes from). Thus, the IR divergences cancel if and only if the external BPs obey
\sum p_{j}=0
(counting incoming and outgoing particles with opposite sign). This is precisely the condition for the amplitude to conserve the transverse momentum, namely to have no recoil of the string. Details of this argument are given in appendix
F
.

It would be interesting to properly take into account the recoil, by expanding around incoming and outgoing states with a general transverse momentum of the string, and to confirm that this indeed cancels the IR divergences we find.

6.4
2 NGB to 2 BP

Finally, the scattering amplitude of two NGBs to two BPs can be computed from the four-point function
\langle X_{0}(-k_{1})X_{0}(-k_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{1},k_{3})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{2},k_{4})\rangle
. It gets contributions from four diagrams (two of which have two different channels).

The first diagram involves the four-point vertex
\mathcal{V}_{2}
, and it splits into two parts due to
\mathcal{V}_{2A},\mathcal{V}_{2B}
, giving:

\begin{split}&\langle X_{0}(k_{1})X_{0}(k_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{1},k_{3})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{2},k_{4})\rangle_{\rm tree,1}=\\

&=\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{4}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}-m^{2}}(2\pi)^{3}\delta(k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\delta(p_{1}+p_{2})\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\frac{i\beta^{2}}{8m}\frac{(k_{1}\cdot(2k_{3}-k_{1}))(k_{2}\cdot(2k_{4}-k_{2}))p_{1}p_{2}}{(k_{3}-k_{1})^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}\\
&+((k_{3},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(k_{4},p_{2}))\\
&+\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{4}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}-m^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\frac{i\pi^{2}\beta^{2}}{32m}\frac{(k_{1}\cdot(2k_{3}-k_{1}))(k_{2}\cdot(2k_{4}-k_{2}))}{(ip_{1}-m)(ip_{2}-m)}\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{1}{(k_{3}-k_{1})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\frac{(p_{1}^{2}-p^{2})(p_{2}^{2}-p^{2})}{p^{2}+m^{2}}{\rm csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}-p)}{2m}\right){\rm csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p_{2}+p)}{2m}\right)\\
&+((k_{3},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(k_{4},p_{2}))\\
&+\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{4}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}-m^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\frac{i\pi\beta^{2}}{16m}\frac{(k_{1}\cdot(2k_{3}-k_{1}))(k_{2}\cdot(2k_{4}-k_{2}))}{(k_{3}-k_{1})^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{p_{1}(p_{2}^{2}-p_{1}^{2})}{(ip_{1}-m)(ip_{2}-m)}\cdot{\rm csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})}{2m}\right)\\
&+((k_{3},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(k_{4},p_{2}))+(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2})+(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2},(k_{3},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(k_{4},p_{2})).\end{split}

(6.18)

The first two summands come from the same vertex (
\mathcal{V}_{3A},\mathcal{V}_{3B}
); therefore, there are two different options when changing the momenta. The third summand comes from two different vertices (one
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
and one
\mathcal{V}_{3B}
); therefore, there are four different options. The integral is evaluated in appendix
G
:

\begin{split}&I^{2}_{\rm 2BP}(t,m,p_{1},p_{2})\equiv\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{1}{t-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\frac{(p_{1}^{2}-p^{2})(p_{2}^{2}-p^{2})}{p^{2}+m^{2}}{\rm csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}-p)}{2m}\right){\rm csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p_{2}+p)}{2m}\right)=\\
&\csch\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})}{2m}\right)\Biggl(\frac{i\left(m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}\right)\left(m^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)\left(-H_{\frac{m+ip_{1}}{2m}}+H_{\frac{m-ip_{1}}{2m}}-H_{\frac{m+ip_{2}}{2m}}+H_{\frac{m-ip_{2}}{2m}}\right)}{2\pi mt}\\
&-\frac{i\left(m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}-t\right)\left(m^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-t\right)\left(-H_{\frac{ip_{1}}{2m}+\frac{\sqrt{-t+m^{2}}}{2m}}+H_{\frac{\sqrt{-t+m^{2}}}{2m}-\frac{ip_{1}}{2m}}-H_{\frac{ip_{2}}{2m}+\frac{\sqrt{-t+m^{2}}}{2m}}+H_{\frac{\sqrt{-t+m^{2}}}{2m}-\frac{ip_{2}}{2m}}\right)}{2\pi t\sqrt{-t+m^{2}}}\\
&+\frac{(p_{1}+p_{2})\left(m^{2}+p_{1}p_{2}-t\right)}{\pi t}\Biggl(1-\frac{m}{\sqrt{-t+m^{2}}}\Biggr)\Biggr).\end{split}

(6.19)

The fourth diagram comes from insertions of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
V_{3BP}
:

\begin{split}&\langle X_{0}(k_{1})X_{0}(k_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{1},k_{3})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{2},k_{4})\rangle_{\rm tree,4}=\\

\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.5pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.09 72.95 L 50.23 121.1}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}}\\
&=\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{4}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}-m^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\frac{i\pi^{2}\beta^{2}}{16m}\frac{(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})}{(ip_{1}-m)(ip_{2}-m)}\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\Biggl(\frac{{\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2}+p)}{2m}\right){\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)}{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\left(3m^{4}+(-p_{1}+p_{2}+p)(p_{1}-p_{2}+p)(p_{1}+p_{2}-p)(p_{1}+p_{2}+p)+2m^{2}(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+p^{2})\right)\Biggr).\end{split}

(6.20)

The integral in the last two lines, denoted as
I^{1}_{\rm 2BP}(s,m,p)
, is evaluated in appendix
G
:

\begin{split}I^{1}_{\rm 2BP}&(s,m,p_{1},p_{2})\\
&\frac{1}{6\pi m}\csch\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})}{2m}\right)\Biggl(-2m(p_{1}+p_{2})\bigl(8m^{2}+5p_{1}^{2}-2p_{1}p_{2}+5p_{2}^{2}-3s\bigr)\\
&-\frac{3im}{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}\left(\left((p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}-s\right)\left((p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}-s\right)-4m^{2}s\right)\cdot\\
&\biggl(2H_{\frac{i(p_{1}+p_{2})}{m}+\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{m}}-H_{\frac{i(p_{1}+p_{2})}{2m}+\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{2m}}-2H_{\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{m}-\frac{i(p_{1}+p_{2})}{m}}+H_{\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{2m}-\frac{i(p_{1}+p_{2})}{2m}}\biggr)\Biggr).\end{split}

(6.21)

The first thing to note is that the
x
-momentum conserving part must vanish. Indeed, onshell, from the first contribution, one has:

\frac{i\beta^{2}}{4m}(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})p_{1}p_{2}=\frac{i\beta^{2}}{8m}sp_{1}p_{2},

(6.22)

while from the third contribution, combining the
t,u
-channels, and using
t=(k_{3}-k_{1})^{2}=p_{1}^{2}+m^{2}-2k_{1}\cdot k_{3},\ u=(k_{4}-k_{1})^{2}=p_{2}^{2}+m^{2}-2k_{1}\cdot k_{4}
:

\begin{split}&\frac{i\beta^{2}}{8m}\frac{(k_{1}\cdot(2k_{3}-k_{1}))(k_{2}\cdot(2k_{4}-k_{2}))p_{1}p_{2}}{(k_{3}-k_{1})^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}+\frac{i\beta^{2}}{8m}\frac{(k_{1}\cdot(2k_{4}-k_{1}))(k_{2}\cdot(2k_{3}-k_{2}))p_{1}p_{2}}{(k_{4}-k_{1})^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}}=\\
&\frac{i\beta^{2}}{8m}p_{1}p_{2}(t-p_{1}^{2}-m^{2}+u-p_{2}^{2}-m^{2}).\end{split}

(6.23)

Adding the two vanishes, due to
s+t+u=\sum_{i=1}^{4}k_{i}^{2}=p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+2m^{2}
.

Going to the scattering amplitude, we put the momenta onshell and use the kinematics
k_{1}\cdot k_{2}=\frac{s}{2}
,
k_{3}\cdot k_{4}=\frac{s-p^{2}-m^{2}}{2}
, and similar expressions for the other multiplications. Energy-Momentum conservation gives:

|\vec{k}_{3}|=|\vec{k}_{4}|=\sqrt{\frac{\left(2m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-s\right)^{2}-4\left(m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}\right)\left(m^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)}{4s}}.

Inserting inside
t,u
yields two symmetric values:

\begin{split}t=\frac{1}{2}\left(2m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-s\pm\sqrt{\left(2m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-s\right)^{2}-4\left(m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}\right)\left(m^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)}\right),\\
u=\frac{1}{2}\left(2m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-s\mp\sqrt{\left(2m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-s\right)^{2}-4\left(m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}\right)\left(m^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)}\right),\end{split}

satisfying
s+t+u=2m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}
. Summing the four contributions gives:

\begin{split}i\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to(p_{1},\vec{k}_{3}),(p_{2},\vec{k}_{4}))=&\frac{i\pi^{2}\beta^{2}s\left(I^{1}_{\rm 2BP}(s,m,p_{1},p_{2})-tI^{2}_{\rm 2BP}(t,m,p_{1},p_{2})-uI^{2}_{\rm 2BP}(u,m,p_{2},p_{1})\right)}{32m(ip_{1}-m)(ip_{2}-m)}\\
-&\frac{i\pi\beta^{2}(p_{1}+p_{2})\left(s\left(4m^{2}+(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}\right)+3\left(p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\right)\csch\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})}{2m}\right)}{48m(ip_{1}-m)(ip_{2}-m)}\\
+&\frac{i\pi^{2}\beta^{2}s(m+ip_{1})(m+ip_{2})\sech\left(\frac{\pi p_{1}}{2m}\right)\sech\left(\frac{\pi p_{2}}{2m}\right)}{32m^{2}}.\end{split}

(6.24)

The total cross section comes from an integration of the scattering amplitude on the allowed phase space:

\begin{split}\sigma_{\rm 2BP}=&\frac{1}{2s}\int\frac{d\vec{k}_{3}dp_{1}}{(2\pi)^{2}\left(2\sqrt{\vec{k}_{3}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+m^{2}}\right)}\frac{d\vec{k}_{4}dp_{2}}{(2\pi)^{2}\left(2\sqrt{\vec{k}_{4}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+m^{2}}\right)}\cdot\\
&\left|\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to(p_{1},\vec{k}_{3}),(p_{2},\vec{k}_{4}))\right|^{2}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4})\delta\left(\sqrt{\vec{k}_{3}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+m^{2}}+\sqrt{\vec{k}_{4}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+m^{2}}-\sqrt{s}\right)\\
=&\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}s}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{s-2\sqrt{s}m}}dp_{1}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{s+p_{1}^{2}-2\sqrt{s}\sqrt{p_{1}^{2}+m^{2}}}}dp_{2}\frac{\left|\mathcal{M}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to(p_{1},\vec{k}_{3}),(p_{2},\vec{k}_{4}))\right|^{2}}{\sqrt{\left(2m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-s\right)^{2}-4\left(m^{2}+p_{1}^{2}\right)\left(m^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)}}.\end{split}

(6.25)

This cross section can not be evaluated analytically, so numerical integration was used. The three finite cross sections are compared in figure
3
, for
m=1,\beta=0.5
(
m\beta^{2}
is small).
\sigma_{\rm 2NGB}
vanishes at
s=0
, and
\sigma_{\rm 2BP}
at
s=4m^{2}
; the latter is due to zero measure of the phase space. This does not happen for
\sigma_{\rm 1BP}
at
s=m^{2}
because the phase space is a point for all
s
.

\sigma_{\rm 2NGB}
decays at large
s
polynomially as
s^{-2}
, while
\sigma_{\rm 1BP}
decays exponentially. Therefore, although
\sigma_{\rm 1BP}
is leading in
\beta
, it must go below
\sigma_{\rm 2NGB}
at some
s(\beta)
. This is expected to hold for the full result, not only for the tree-level approximation.

Figure 3:
Different total cross sections for scattering of two NGBs as a function of the COM energy
s
(in units of
m^{2}
); to one BP, two NGBs, and two BPs, for
m=1,\beta=0.5
.

7
Conclusions

In this paper we made first steps towards analyzing the effective action on confining strings in theories like QED
3
, for which the mass gap
m
in the bulk is much smaller than the scale set by the string tension. Above the scale
m
, the effective action must include both the NGB on the string and the massive bulk particle, and we found that this leads to several changes in the properties of the strings.

In section
4
we found that the width of the string changes from the usual effective string behavior (where the width is dominated by quantum fluctuations) for exponentially long strings, to the classical width (of order
1/m
, independent of the length) when the strings are long compared to the string tension scale, but not exponentially long. It would be interesting to observe this transition in lattice simulations of QED-like theories. However, as we discussed in the introduction, it is challenging to significantly separate the scales of the mass, of the tension, and of the lattice spacing on a finite lattice.

In section
5
we computed the ground state energy of the string, finding large corrections to the usual EST results when the length
L
of the string is of order
1/m
or smaller. It would be interesting to compute the corrections also to other energy levels, and to measure the ground state energy and other energies in lattice simulations. We expect qualitatively similar results for other energy levels below
m
, while energy levels above
m
can mix with the bulk particles and decay into them.

In section
6
we computed (at leading order in perturbation theory) the scattering amplitudes of two NGBs. Somewhat surprisingly, we found that the scattering amplitudes to final states including BPs and NGBs exhibit IR divergences that need to be regularized, related to the massless NGBs. Usually NGBs do not give rise to IR divergences because their couplings are proportional to their momenta, but it turns out that when they scatter into bulk fields, this is not enough to eliminate the IR divergences. We argued that, unlike in other theories of massless particles, the IR divergences here are not related to a dressing of the massive particles by soft massless particles. Instead, we believe that these IR divergences are related to the recoil of the string when it emits or absorbs bulk particles carrying transverse momentum, forced by the momentum conservation of the full theory, which we did not take into account in our computations. It would be interesting to understand better how to take this recoil into account, and to confirm that doing this cancels the IR divergences that we find, and leads to finite cross sections.

There are various possible generalizations of our work. Our analysis essentially uses just the fact that the low-energy action of QED
3
(with any matter content, as long as monopole-instantons are allowed) is the sine-Gordon theory. It would be interesting to generalize the analysis to other weakly coupled low-energy theories, such as the massive
\phi^{4}
theory, where we expect to get extra modes living on the string (since the
\phi^{4}
soliton in
1+1
dimensions has an extra bound state below the continuum). It would also be interesting to study examples in
3+1
dimensions in which the bulk mass gap is well below the string tension scale.

A final remark concerns the Lorentz symmetry, which is not manifest in our action (in both formalisms). In general, effective actions on string-like objects are constrained by Lorentz symmetry, which rotates/boosts the string, and acts non-linearly on the NGBs describing its transverse position. In our case, when we also include the bulk mode
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}
in the effective action, Lorentz transformations mix the NGB with
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}
in a complicated non-linear way. We expect that order by order in perturbation theory Lorentz symmetry will give some constraints on the effective action, but we leave their analysis to future work. The specific effective action that we compute is definitely Lorentz-invariant, since we derive it from an underlying Lorentz-invariant theory.

Appendix A
Fluctuations Above the String

This appendix summarizes the orthonormality and completeness relations among the wave-functions presented in subsection
3.1
. We consider the
x
-axis only; the
y,t
-axes are trivial. Using bra-ket notation, the orthonormality and completeness relation of the
x
-basis are written as:

\begin{split}\braket{x_{1}|x_{2}}&=\delta(x_{1}-x_{2}),\qquad\int dx\ket{x}\bra{x}=\hat{I}.\end{split}

(A.1)

For the other continuous basis
\ket{p}
(ignoring for a moment the discrete NGB mode), there are three coefficients among these relations and the matrix-elements between the
\ket{x},\ket{p}
bases:

\begin{split}\braket{p_{1}|p_{2}}&=C_{1}(p_{1})\delta(p_{1}-p_{2}),\\
\int dpC_{2}(p)\ket{p}\bra{p}&=\hat{I},\\
\braket{x|p}&=C_{3}(p)f_{p}(x),\\
\int dxf_{p_{1}}(x)f_{p_{2}}^{*}(x)&=N(p_{1})\delta(p_{1}-p_{2}).\end{split}

(A.2)

Consistency between these relations gives:

C_{1}(p)C_{2}(p)=1,\qquad N(p)|C_{3}(p)|^{2}=C_{1}(p).

(A.3)

For
f_{p}=\phi_{p}
, we have
N(p)=2\pi
. If we choose
C_{3}(p)=1
then
C_{1}(p)=2\pi,C_{2}(p)=\frac{1}{2\pi}
, and this was used all everywhere in this paper.

Adding the NGB mode
\ket{0}
, the relations read:

\begin{split}&\braket{p_{1}|p_{2}}=2\pi\delta(p_{1}-p_{2}),\ \braket{p|0}=0,\ \braket{0|0}=1,\\
&\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\ket{p}\bra{p}+\ket{0}\bra{0}=\hat{I},\\
&\braket{x|p}=\phi_{p}(x),\ \braket{x|0}=\phi_{0}(x).\end{split}

(A.4)

Using the wave-functions, these relations reads:

\begin{split}&\int dx\phi_{p_{1}}(x)\phi_{q}^{*}(x)=2\pi\delta(p-q),\ \int dx\phi_{p}(x)\phi_{0}(x)=0,\ \int dx\phi_{0}^{2}(x)=1,\\
&\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\phi_{p}(x_{1})\phi_{p}^{*}(x_{2})+\phi_{0}(x_{1})\phi_{0}(x_{2})=\delta(x_{1}-x_{2}).\end{split}

(A.5)

Appendix B
Change of Variables

By comparing (
3.1
) and (
3.18
) to the expansion of (
3.33
) around
x_{0}=0
, one can write the change of variables between the NGB
\varphi_{0}(\sigma)
and the BP
\varphi(p,\sigma)
in the direct formalism and the NGB
X_{0}(\sigma)
and the BP
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)
in the stringy formalism:

\begin{split}\phi_{\rm cl}(x)+\delta\phi_{\rm GB}(x,\sigma)+\delta\phi_{\rm BP}(x,\sigma)&=\phi_{\rm cl}(x)+\phi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(\sigma)+\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\phi_{p}^{*}(x)\varphi(p,\sigma)\\
=\phi_{\rm cl}(x-x_{0}(\sigma))+\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x-x_{0}(\sigma),\sigma)&=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\frac{d^{n}\phi_{\rm cl}(x)}{dx^{n}}x_{0}^{n}(\sigma)+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\frac{d^{n}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x)}{dx^{n}}x_{0}^{n}(\sigma)\\
&=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\frac{d^{n}\phi_{\rm cl}(x)}{dx^{n}}x_{0}^{n}(\sigma)+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{d^{n}\phi_{p}^{*}(x)}{dx^{n}}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)x_{0}^{n}(\sigma).\end{split}

(B.1)

The
n=0
term of the first series is just
\phi_{\rm cl}(x)
, which cancels on both sides. Using the orthonormality relations of
\phi_{0}(x),\phi_{p}(x)
one finds for
\varphi_{0}(\sigma)
:

\begin{split}\varphi_{0}(\sigma)=&\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\left(\int dx\phi_{0}(x)\frac{d^{n}\phi_{\rm cl}(x)}{dx^{n}}\right)x_{0}^{n}(\sigma)+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(\int dx\phi_{0}(x)\frac{d^{n}\phi_{p}^{*}(x)}{dx^{n}}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)x_{0}^{n}(\sigma)\\
=&-X_{0}(\sigma)+\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}c_{1n}\beta^{n-1}X_{0}^{n}(\sigma)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}c_{2n}(p)\beta^{n}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)X_{0}^{n}(\sigma),\\
c_{1n}\equiv&\frac{1}{n!}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8m}}\right)^{n}\left(\int dx\phi_{0}(x)\frac{d^{n}(\beta\phi_{\rm cl}(x))}{dx^{n}}\right),\ c_{2n}(p)\equiv\frac{1}{n!}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8m}}\right)^{n}\int dx\phi_{0}(x)\frac{d^{n}\phi_{p}^{*}(x)}{dx^{n}}.\end{split}

(B.2)

For
\varphi(p,\sigma)
one finds:

\begin{split}\varphi(p,\sigma)=&\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\left(\int dx\phi_{p}(x)\frac{d^{n}\phi_{\rm cl}(x)}{dx^{n}}\right)x_{0}^{n}(\sigma)+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\int\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\left(\int dx\phi_{p}(x)\frac{d^{n}\phi_{p^{\prime}}^{*}(x)}{dx^{n}}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p^{\prime},\sigma)x_{0}^{n}(\sigma)\\
=&\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)+\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}c_{3n}\beta^{n-1}X_{0}^{n}(\sigma)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\int\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}c_{4n}(p^{\prime})\beta^{n}X_{0}^{n}(\sigma)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p^{\prime},\sigma),\\
c_{3n}\equiv&\frac{1}{n!}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8m}}\right)^{n}\left(\int dx\phi_{p}(x)\frac{d^{n}(\beta\phi_{\rm cl}(x))}{dx^{n}}\right),\ c_{4n}(p^{\prime})\equiv\frac{1}{n!}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8m}}\right)^{n}\int dx\phi_{p}(x)\frac{d^{n}\phi_{p^{\prime}}^{*}(x)}{dx^{n}}.\end{split}

(B.3)

Hence, both variables are the same to order
\beta^{0}
, and deviate by terms of order
\beta^{>0}
. Using states and operators language, one can write the relations in a compact form (note that
\ket{p}
is not an eigenvector of the
x
-momentum operator
\hat{P}
):

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=-X_{0}(\sigma)+\bra{\phi_{\rm cl}}\left(e^{-i\hat{P}x_{0}(\sigma)}+i\hat{P}x_{0}(\sigma)-\hat{I}\right)\ket{0}+\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)\bra{p}e^{-i\hat{P}x_{0}(\sigma)}\ket{0},

(B.4)

\displaystyle\varphi(p,\sigma)

\displaystyle=\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)+\bra{\phi_{\rm cl}}\left(e^{-i\hat{P}x_{0}(\sigma)}+i\hat{P}x_{0}(\sigma)-\hat{I}\right)\ket{p}+\int\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p^{\prime},\sigma)\bra{p^{\prime}}\left(e^{-i\hat{P}x_{0}(\sigma)}-\hat{I}\right)\ket{p}.

(B.5)

Appendix C
Vertices in Pseudo-Momentum Space

In this appendix, we evaluate the vertices needed in this work in pseudo-momentum space, for the two formalisms. For each vertex,
\sigma
-integrals and
\varphi_{0}/x_{0}
-dependencies will be suppressed along the calculations, and will be added after performing the
x
-integration.

C.1
Direct Formalism

V_{2NGB,1BP}
:

\begin{split}V_{2NGB,1BP}\subset&-\frac{m^{2}\beta}{3!}\int dx(-2\sech(mx)\tanh(mx))\delta\phi^{3}\\
\propto&\frac{2m^{2}\beta}{3!}3\int dx\sech(mx)\tanh(mx)\phi_{0}^{2}(x)\phi_{p}^{*}(x)\\
=&\frac{\pi\beta}{16m}(m-ip)(m^{2}+p^{2})\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right).\end{split}

(C.1)

The vertex is then:

\begin{split}V_{2NGB,1BP}=&\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\cdot\\
&\frac{\pi\beta}{16m}(m-ip)(m^{2}+p^{2})\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\varphi(p,q_{1})\varphi_{0}(q_{2})\varphi_{0}(q_{3}).\end{split}

(C.2)

V_{4NGB}
:

\begin{split}V_{4NGB}\subset&-\frac{m^{2}\beta^{2}}{4!}\int dx(1-2\sech^{2}(mx))\delta\phi^{4}\\
\propto&-\frac{m^{2}\beta^{2}}{4!}\int dx(1-2\sech^{2}(mx))\phi_{0}^{4}(x)=\frac{m^{3}\beta^{2}}{4!\cdot 5}.\end{split}

(C.3)

The vertex is then:

\begin{split}V_{4NGB}=\frac{m^{3}\beta^{2}}{4!\cdot 5}\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}+q_{4}).\end{split}

(C.4)

C.2
Stringy Formalism

\mathcal{V}_{1}
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{1}\propto&-\frac{2m^{2}}{\beta}\intop dx\sech(mx){\rm tanh}(mx)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x)\\
=&-\frac{2m^{2}}{\beta}\intop dx\sech(mx){\rm tanh}(mx)\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}e^{-ipx}\frac{ip+m\tanh(mx)}{ip+m}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p)\\
=&-\frac{\pi}{\beta}\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(m-ip\right)\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p),\end{split}

(C.5)

so
19
19

19

One can also evaluate it by using the vertex with the derivative on
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}
.
:

\mathcal{V}_{1}=-\frac{\pi}{\beta}\frac{\beta^{2}}{8m}\int d^{2}\sigma(\partial_{a}X_{0}(\sigma))^{2}\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(m-ip\right)\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma).

(C.6)

Finally:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{1}=\frac{\pi\beta}{8m}&\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(q_{1}\cdot q_{2})(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\cdot\\
&\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(m-ip\right)\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)x_{0}(q_{1})x_{0}(q_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,q_{3}).\end{split}

(C.7)

\mathcal{V}_{2}
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{2}\propto&\frac{1}{2}\intop dx\left(\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x)\right)^{2}\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2}\right)\cdot\\
&\intop dxe^{-ix\left(p_{2}+p_{1}\right)}\cdot\left(\left(-ip_{1}+\partial_{x}\right)\left(\frac{ip_{1}+m\tanh(mx)}{ip_{1}+m}\right)\right)\cdot\left(1\to 2\right).\end{split}

(C.8)

When we distribute the expression, written schematically as
\left(-ip_{1}+\partial_{x}\right)\left(-ip_{2}+\partial_{x}\right)
,
we get four terms. The term proportional to
\left(-ip_{1}\right)\left(-ip_{2}\right)

can be simplified using eigenfunction orthogonality, giving the momentum
conserving part:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{2A}=&\frac{1}{2}\frac{\beta^{2}}{8m}\int d^{2}\sigma(\partial_{a}X_{0})^{2}\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}p^{2}\left(\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\right)^{2}\Rightarrow\\
\mathcal{V}_{2A}=&\frac{\beta^{2}}{16m}\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(-q_{1}\cdot q_{2})(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}+q_{4})\cdot\\
&\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}p^{2}x_{0}(q_{1})x_{0}(q_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,q_{3})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(-p,q_{4}).\end{split}

(C.9)

The remaining three terms are:

\begin{split}&m^{2}\sech^{2}(mx)\left(p^{2}_{2}-ip_{2}m\tanh(mx)\right)+m^{2}\sech^{2}(mx)\left(p^{2}_{1}-ip_{1}m\tanh(mx)\right)+m^{4}\sech^{4}(mx)=\\
&m^{2}\sech^{2}(mx)\left(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)-im^{3}\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)\sech^{2}(mx)\tanh(mx)+m^{4}\sech^{4}(mx).\end{split}

(C.10)

The integral over
x
is then:

\begin{split}&\intop dxe^{-ix\left(p_{2}+p_{1}\right)}\frac{m^{2}\sech^{2}(mx)\left(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)-im^{3}\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)\sech^{2}(mx)\tanh(mx)+m^{4}\sech^{4}(mx)}{\left(ip_{1}+m\right)\left(ip_{2}+m\right)}\\
&\qquad\qquad=\frac{\frac{2\pi}{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}^{2}-p_{1}p_{2}+p_{2}^{2}+m^{2})\csch\left(\frac{\pi\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}{2m}\right)}{(ip_{1}+m)(ip_{2}+m)}.\end{split}

(C.11)

The vertex is then:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{2B}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\beta^{2}}{8m}&\int d^{2}\sigma(\partial_{a}X_{0}(\sigma))^{2}\cdot\\
&\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\frac{\frac{2\pi}{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}^{2}-p_{1}p_{2}+p_{2}^{2}+m^{2})\csch\left(\frac{\pi\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}{2m}\right)}{(ip_{1}+m)(ip_{2}+m)}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1},\sigma\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2},\sigma\right)\Rightarrow\\
\mathcal{V}_{2B}=\frac{\beta^{2}}{16m}&\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(-q_{1}\cdot q_{2})(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}+q_{4})\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\cdot\\
&\frac{\frac{2\pi}{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}^{2}-p_{1}p_{2}+p_{2}^{2}+m^{2})\csch\left(\frac{\pi\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}{2m}\right)}{(ip_{1}+m)(ip_{2}+m)}x_{0}(q_{1})x_{0}(q_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1},q_{3}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2},q_{4}\right).\end{split}

(C.12)

\mathcal{V}_{3}
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{3}\propto&-\intop dx\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\partial_{a}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\\
=&-\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1}\right)\partial_{a}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2}\right)\cdot\\
&\intop dxe^{-ix\left(p_{2}+p_{1}\right)}\left(\left(-ip_{1}+\partial_{x}\right)\left(\frac{ip_{1}+m\tanh(mx)}{ip_{1}+m}\right)\right)\left(\frac{ip_{2}+m\tanh(mx)}{ip_{2}+m}\right).\end{split}

(C.13)

From the
-ip_{1}
factor, using orthogonality, we get the momentum-conserving vertex:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{3A}=&-\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\int d^{2}\sigma\partial_{a}X_{0}\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(-ip\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p\right)\partial_{a}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(-p\right)\Rightarrow\\
\mathcal{V}_{3A}=&\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(-q_{1}\cdot q_{3})(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(ip\right)x_{0}(q_{1})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p,q_{2}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(-p,q_{3}\right).\end{split}

(C.14)

This form is not symmetric in the two BP fields. Using momentum conservation, one can modify it to:

\mathcal{V}_{3A}=\frac{\beta}{2\sqrt{8m}}\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(q_{1}\cdot(q_{2}-q_{3}))(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\left(ip\right)x_{0}(q_{1})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p,q_{2}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(-p,q_{3}\right).

(C.15)

The
q_{1}^{2}
term vanishes because it is anti-symmetric with respect to the change of integration variables
q_{2}\leftrightarrow q_{3}
together with
p\to-p
due to the additional
p
factor.

From the other term, we get:

\begin{split}-\intop dxe^{-ix\left(p_{2}+p_{1}\right)}\frac{m^{2}\sech^{2}(mx)}{ip_{1}+m}\frac{ip_{2}+m\tanh(mx)}{ip_{2}+m}=\frac{\frac{i\pi}{2}\left(p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)\csch\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})}{2m}\right)}{(ip_{1}+m)(ip_{2}+m)},\end{split}

(C.16)

so we find:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{3B}=&\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\int d^{2}\sigma\partial_{a}X_{0}\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\cdot\\
&\frac{\frac{i\pi}{2}\left(p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)}{\left(ip_{1}+m\right)\left(ip_{2}+m\right)}\csch\left(\frac{\pi\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}{2m}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1}\right)\partial_{a}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2}\right)\Rightarrow\\
\mathcal{V}_{3B}=&\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(-q_{1}\cdot q_{3})(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\cdot\\
&\frac{\frac{i\pi}{2}\left(p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)}{\left(ip_{1}+m\right)\left(ip_{2}+m\right)}\csch\left(\frac{\pi\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}{2m}\right)x_{0}(q_{1})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1},q_{2}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2},q_{3}\right).\end{split}

(C.17)

Again, this form is not symmetric in the two BP fields. Using momentum conservation, one can modify it to:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{3B}=&\frac{i\pi\beta}{4\sqrt{8m}}\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(q_{1}\cdot(q_{2}-q_{3}))(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\cdot\\
&\frac{\left(p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)}{\left(ip_{1}+m\right)\left(ip_{2}+m\right)}\csch\left(\frac{\pi\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}{2m}\right)x_{0}(q_{1})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1},q_{2}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2},q_{3}\right).\end{split}

(C.18)

The
q_{1}^{2}
term vanishes as before because it is anti-symmetric with respect to the change of integration variables
q_{2}\leftrightarrow q_{3},\ p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
, due to the additional
p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}
factor.

V_{3BP}
:

\begin{split}V_{3BP}\propto&\intop dx\tanh(mx)\sech(mx)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}^{3}\\
=&\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{3}}{2\pi}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{1}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{2}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(p_{3}\right)\cdot\\
&\intop dxe^{-ix\left(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}\right)}\tanh(mx)\sech(mx)\cdot\\
&\left(\frac{ip_{1}+m\tanh(mx)}{ip_{1}+m}\right)\left(\frac{ip_{2}+m\tanh(mx)}{ip_{2}+m}\right)\left(\frac{ip_{3}+m\tanh(mx)}{ip_{3}+m}\right)\\
=&\frac{\pi}{8m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{2m}\right)\cdot\\
&\frac{3m^{4}+(-p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{1}-p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})+2m^{2}(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+p_{3}^{2})}{(ip_{1}+m)(ip_{2}+m)(ip_{3}+m)}.\end{split}

(C.19)

Hence:

\begin{split}V_{3BP}=&\frac{2m^{2}\beta}{3!}\intop d^{2}\sigma\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{3}}{2\pi}\cdot\\
&\frac{3m^{4}+(-p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{1}-p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})+2m^{2}(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+p_{3}^{2})}{(ip_{1}+m)(ip_{2}+m)(ip_{3}+m)}\cdot\\
&\frac{\pi}{8m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{2m}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{1},\sigma)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{2},\sigma)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{3},\sigma)\Rightarrow\\
V_{3BP}=&\frac{\pi m\beta}{24}\int\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{d^{2}q_{i}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\right)(2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\intop\frac{dp_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{2}}{2\pi}\frac{dp_{3}}{2\pi}\cdot\\
&\frac{3m^{4}+(-p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{1}-p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})+2m^{2}(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+p_{3}^{2})}{(ip_{1}+m)(ip_{2}+m)(ip_{3}+m)}\cdot\\
&\sech\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{2m}\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{1},q_{1})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{2},q_{2})\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{3},q_{3}).\end{split}

(C.20)

Appendix D
Computation of the Jacobian

In this appendix, we compute the Jacobian of the change of variables
\phi
to
X_{0}
and
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}
. It will be convenient to use the spectral decomposition coefficients
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)
as variables instead of
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x,\sigma)
since the latter are constrained by orthogonality to
\sech(m(x-x_0(\sg)))
. So we need:

\begin{split}1=&\int\mathcal{D}X_{0}(\sigma)\mathcal{D}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)J[X_{0},\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}]\delta(\phi(x,\sigma)-\phi[X_{0}(\sigma),\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)]),\\
J=&\int\mathcal{D}\psi_{0}(\sigma_{1})\mathcal{D}\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{1})\mathcal{D}\chi(x,\sigma_{2})e^{iS_{g}}\equiv e^{iS_{J}},\\
S_{g}\equiv&-i\int dxd^{2}\sigma_{2}d^{2}\sigma_{1}\chi(x,\sigma_{2})\left(\psi_{0}(\sigma_{1})\frac{\delta\phi(x,\sigma_{2})}{\delta X_{0}(\sigma_{1})}+\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{1})\frac{\delta\phi(x,\sigma_{2})}{\delta(\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{1}))}\right).\end{split}

(D.1)

After we insert the Faddeev-Popov identity into the PI, the
\mathcal{D}\phi(x,\sigma)
integration removes the
\delta(\phi-\phi[X_{0},\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}])
, the ghost will disappear, and we remain with
\mathcal{D}X_{0}(\sigma)\mathcal{D}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)
integration. Alternatively, we could simply use
\mathcal{D}\phi(x,\sigma)=J\mathcal{D}X_{0}(\sigma)\mathcal{D}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)
. However, the ghosts are utilized to present the operators in the determinant in a cleaner way.
20
20

20

The exponent in
J
in a discretized notation, using a lattice with
N^{3}
sites, is
\chi_{i}A_{ij}\psi_{0,j}+\chi_{i}A_{ik}\psi_{\rm bulk,k}
, where the second index splits into two:
1\leq i\leq N^{3},\ 1\leq j\leq N^{2},\ 1\leq k\leq(N-1)N^{2}
. This separation amounts to the separation of the NGB and the BP. Using the ghosts, we will bring it to the form
\chi_{i}A_{ij}\psi_{j}
, and then
J=\det(A)
.

Using:

\begin{split}\frac{\delta\phi(x,\sigma_{2})}{\delta(\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{1}))}&=\phi_{p}^{*}(x)\delta(\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2}),\\
\frac{\delta\phi(x,\sigma_{2})}{\delta X_{0}(\sigma_{1})}&=-\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\partial_{x}\phi(x-x_{0}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2})\delta(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}),\end{split}

(D.2)

the ghost variation is:

\begin{split}&\int d^{2}\sigma_{1}\left(\psi_{0}(\sigma_{1})\frac{\delta\phi(x,\sigma_{2})}{\delta X_{0}(\sigma_{1})}+\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{1})\frac{\delta\phi(x,\sigma_{2})}{\delta(\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{1}))}\right)\\
=&-\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\partial_{x}\phi(x,\sigma_{2})\psi_{0}(\sigma_{2})+\frac{dp}{2\pi}\phi_{p}^{*}(x-x_{0}(\sigma_{2}))\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{2})\\
=&\left(-\frac{2m}{\beta}\sech(m(x-x_0(\sg_2)))-\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x-x_{0}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2})\right)\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\psi_{0}(\sigma_{2})\\
&+\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\phi_{p}^{*}(x-x_{0}(\sigma_{2}))\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{2}).\end{split}

(D.3)

So the ghost action can be written compactly as:

S_{g}=-i\int dxd^{2}\sigma\chi(x+x_{0}(\sigma),\sigma)\left(-\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\psi_{0}(\sigma)(\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x,\sigma)+\frac{2m}{\beta}\sech(mx))+\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\phi_{p}^{*}(x)\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma_{2})\right).

(D.4)

We combine the ghosts
\psi_{0}(\sigma),\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)
into a single ghost
\psi(x,\sigma)
:

\begin{split}\psi(x,\sigma)&=\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\phi_{p}^{*}(x)\psi_{\rm bulk}(p,\sigma)-\sqrt{\frac{m}{2}}\sech(mx)\psi_{0}(\sigma)\\
\Rightarrow\psi_{0}(\sigma)&=-\sqrt{\frac{m}{2}}\int dx^{\prime}\sech(mx')\psi(x^{\prime},\sigma).\end{split}

(D.5)

This transformation has trivial Jacobian due to the same orthogonality considerations as in the direct formalism. Then, the ghost action reads:

\begin{split}S_{g}&=-i\int dxd^{2}\sigma dx^{\prime}d^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\chi(x+x_{0}(\sigma),\sigma)\mathcal{A}(x,x^{\prime};\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\psi(x^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}),\\
\mathcal{A}(x,x^{\prime};\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})&\equiv\left(\delta(x-x^{\prime})+\frac{\beta}{4}\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x,\sigma)\sech(mx')\right)\delta(\sigma-\sigma^{\prime})\\
&\equiv I_{3d}+\mathcal{B}.\end{split}

(D.6)

One more step is needed, to remove
x_{0}(\sigma)
from the argument of
\chi
:

\begin{split}\chi(x+x_{0}(\sigma),\sigma)&=e^{x_{0}(\sigma)\partial_{x}}\chi(x,\sigma)\\
&=\int dx^{\prime}d\sigma^{\prime}\delta(x-x^{\prime})\delta(\sigma-\sigma^{\prime})e^{x_{0}(\sigma^{\prime})\partial_{x^{\prime}}}\chi(x^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})\\
&\equiv\int dx^{\prime}d^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\mathcal{E}(x,\sigma;x^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})\chi(x^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}).\end{split}

(D.7)

The total Jacobian factor is:

\begin{split}J=&\int\mathcal{D}\psi(x^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})\mathcal{D}\chi(x,\sigma)\exp(\int dxd^2\sg dx'd^2\sg'dx''d^2\sg''\chi(x,\sg) \EE^T(x,\sg;x',\sg')\AAA(x',\sg';x'',\sg'')\psi(x'',\sg''))\\
=&\det(\EE)\det(\AAA),\end{split}

(D.8)

Since the eigenvalues of
\partial_{x}
come in pairs
\pm p
, we have
\det(\EE)=1
. This will be true in any regularization that has
x
-axis translation and reflection symmetries. This triviality is ultimately because a
\sigma
-dependent
x
-translation is also an orthogonal transformation.
Using
\det(\AAA)=e^{\log\det(\AAA)}=e^{\Tr\log(\AAA)}
, the other determinant gives:

\begin{split}\Tr[\log(\AAA)]=&\Tr[\log(I_{3d} + \BB)]\\
=&-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr(-\BB)^{n}\\
=&-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left(-\frac{\beta}{4}\right)^{n}\delta^{2}(0)\int d^{2}\sigma\prod_{i=1}^{n}dx_{i}\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x_{i},\sigma)\sech(mx_{i+1\mod n})\\
=&-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left(-\frac{\beta}{4}\right)^{n}\delta^{2}(0)\int d^{2}\sigma\left(\int dx\sech(mx)\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x,\sigma)\right)^{n}\\
=&\delta^{2}(0)\int d^{2}\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\beta}{4}\int dx\sech(mx)\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x,\sigma)\right).\end{split}

(D.9)

These are a series of counterterms, due to the UV divergence in
\delta^{2}(0)
, for
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}
. Finally, we find:

\begin{split}S_{J}=&-i\log(J)\\
=&i\delta^{2}(0)\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left(-\frac{\beta}{4}\right)^{n}\int d^{2}\sigma\left(\int dx\sech(mx)\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(x,\sigma)\right)^{n}.\end{split}

(D.10)

Appendix E
Scattering in the Direct Formalism

In this appendix, we calculate the two NGBs to one BP and two NGBs to two NGBs scattering amplitudes in the direct formalism. The results obtained are the same as those calculated in section
6
using the stringy formalism. The two NGBs to one BP amplitude involves one diagram, as simple as the stringy formalism. The two NGBs to NGBs amplitude involves four diagrams rather than three in the stringy formalism, and the integral inside is more cumbersome. To be cautious, offshell correlators will be calculated first, and the scattering amplitudes will be deduced from them. While onshell scattering amplitudes match, offshell correlators differ. This is expected since the fields themselves differ. This offshell difference explains how the onshell quantities should match. From the examples calculated in this appendix, this observation can be seen in two facts:

1.

Although the NGB does not only have derivative interactions in the direct formalism, as a NGB should, they appear in the scattering amplitude when putting the momenta onshell.

2.

The two NGBs to two NGBs amplitude in the stringy formalism got contributions from
s,t,u
-channels with an intermediate BP (and because of the kinematics, the
t
-channel vanished). In the direct formalism, these three diagrams contribute (and now the
t
-channel does not vanish), in addition to a quartic NGB diagram.
21
21

21

In principle, this formalism allows another three diagrams with an intermediate NGB, but
V_{3NGB}
vanishes due to
x
-reflection symmetry.

This appendix is also a nice manifestation of the fact that scattering amplitudes are physical, i.e., independent of the fields used to describe the particles and their interactions. We will use the vertices for the direct formalism calculated in appendix
C
.

E.1
2 NGB to 1 BP

The correlator
\langle\varphi_{0}(k_{1})\varphi_{0}(k_{2})\varphi(p,k_{3})\rangle
has a tree-level contribution from one diagram containing the vertex
V_{2NGB,1BP}
:

\begin{split}&\langle\varphi_{0}(-k_{1})\varphi_{0}(-k_{2})\varphi(p,k_{3})\rangle_{\rm tree}=\vbox{\hbox{

}}\\
&=\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(-k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3})\left(-\frac{i\pi\beta}{8m}\right)(m+ip)(m^{2}+p^{2})\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right).\end{split}

(E.1)

The correlation function is not the same as the correlator in (
6.1
). The scattering amplitude is the pole, and onshell,
s=(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}=p^{2}+m^{2}
:

iT(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2}\to(p,\vec{k}_{3}))=-\frac{i\pi\beta s}{8m}(m\pm i\sqrt{s-m^{2}}){\rm sech}\left(\frac{\pi\sqrt{s-m^{2}}}{2m}\right)(2\pi)^{2}\delta(-k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3}).

(E.2)

This is the same as (
6.2
) up to a minus sign. This minus appears because of the relative sign between the fields representing the NGB,
\varphi_{0}=-X_{0}+O(\beta)
, see (
B.2
).

E.2
2 NGB to 2 NGB

For two NGBs to two NGB scattering, there are four diagrams, one of four NGBs with insertion of
V_{4NGB}
, and three with intermediate BP in
s,t,u
channels with insertions of two
V_{2NGB,1BP}
’s (accompanied with a factor of
1/2
). The first one contributes:

}}\\
&=\frac{i}{k_{1}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{2}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{3}^{2}}\frac{i}{k_{4}^{2}}(2\pi)^{2}\delta(-k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4})\left(-\frac{i\pi^{2}\beta^{2}}{64m^{2}}\right)\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{(m^{2}+p^{2})^{3}}{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\sech^{2}\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\\
&+(k_{1}\leftrightarrow-k_{3})+(k_{1}\leftrightarrow-k_{3},k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2}).\end{split}

(E.4)

This integral is evaluated in appendix
G
, and the result is:

\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{(m^{2}+p^{2})^{3}}{s-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\sech^{2}\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)=-\frac{2m^{5}}{15\pi^{2}}\left(32+\frac{20s}{m^{2}}+\frac{15s^{2}}{m^{4}}\right)-\frac{s^{3}}{\pi^{2}}\frac{\psi\left(1,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}{2m}\right)}{\sqrt{-s+m^{2}}}.

(E.5)

Adding the three channels onshell, the constant term
\left(-\frac{i\pi^{2}\beta^{2}}{64m^{2}}\right)3\left(-\frac{64m^{5}}{15\pi^{2}}\right)=\frac{im^{3}\beta^{2}}{5}
cancels the quartic vertex contribution. The linear term in
s
cancels between the
s
and the
u
channels. All the rest give the same answer as (
6.6
).

Appendix F
IR divergences by Soft NGBs

In this appendix, we give more details for the argument presented in subsection
6.3
, regarding the cancellation of the IR divergences from emitted soft NGBs.

The process that gives rise to an IR divergence is the insertion of
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
on a BP’s external leg. When the NGB becomes soft, the BP’s internal propagator becomes onshell and cancels the momentum factor coming from the derivative interactions of the NGB. Then, in the total cross section, an IR divergence appears due to the phase space factor of the NGB,
\frac{d\vec{k}}{2|\vec{k}|}
. We claim that this divergence is not related to the dressing of the BP by NGBs but to a recoil of the string. This suggestion relies on two observations discussed in the following two subsections.

F.1
One-Loop Amplitudes

The first observation is that the one-loop correction to the BP propagator does not have an IR divergence. There are two diagrams involving a NGB’s loop:

\begin{split}&\langle\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(p_{1},k)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}(-p_{2},-k)\rangle_{\rm 1-loop}=\\

(F.1)

One can easily check that the momentum-conserving part
p=p_{1}=p_{2}
does not contain any IR divergences: both corrections

\displaystyle\left(\frac{i\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\right)^{2}(ip)^{2}\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{i(-q\cdot(2k-q))}{q^{2}+i\epsilon}\frac{iq\cdot(2k-q)}{(k-q)^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}+\frac{i\beta^{2}}{8m}p^{2}\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{iq^{2}}{q^{2}+i\epsilon}

(F.2)

do not diverge as
q\to 0
. The non-conserving part also does not contain any IR divergences. For
\mathcal{V}_{2B}
, the loop integral is the same. For insertion of one
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
and one
\mathcal{V}_{3B}
or two
\mathcal{V}_{3B}
’s, the factor
(q\cdot(2k-q))^{2}
remains in the numerator and makes the limit
q\to 0
finite.
22
22

22

Even when the momentum

(p,k)
is onshell and one considers the momentum-conserving part, the result is IR finite; in particular, it vanishes (using some UV regularization maintaining reflection symmetry in
\sigma
directions). This is expected, as should any other momentum-conserving correction, which is present everywhere in the bulk; see comment in subsection
3.2.3
. The two-
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
diagram gives
\frac{\beta^{2}p^{2}}{8m}\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}
and the
\mathcal{V}_{2A}
diagram gives the opposite value.

In addition, if the BP should be dressed with the NGB, there should be an IR divergence in the
1
-loop correction to the process of two NGBs to one BP, that cancels the IR divergence for the tree-level process of two NGBs to one BP and one NGB (discussed in subsection
6.3
), when the latter NGB is soft with energy below the detection accuracy. However, it seems that no IR divergences appear in the loop correction; the loop integral is finite near the
q\to 0
region, where
q
is the momentum of the NGB running in the loop. Showing this requires a lengthy calculation, which will not be performed here. For a handwaving argument, note that usually (as in QED for example) one can cut the soft particle propagator in the IR-divergent
1
-loop diagram, omit one part of the broken propagator, and obtain the tree-level IR-divergent diagram with the second part as a soft particle emitted. In our case, it is easy to see that none of the loop diagrams can produce the tree-level diagram in this way. There are six
1
-loop diagrams involving a NGB:

(F.3)

Out of them, cutting the NGB such that it connects to a BP propagator is possible in the first four. They give the scattering process of two NGBs to one BP and one NPB, but in the
t,u
channels and not in the
s
-channel.

F.2
Recoil of the String

The second observation is that the IR divergence vanishes when the
x
-momentum of the BPs is conserved, and the string does not recoil. For the scattering of two NGBs to one BP and one NGB discussed in subsection
6.3
, this can be seen from the
p
factor appearing in the first term of (
6.14
). This part contains the
s
-channel from the
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
vertex, and it is finite when
\vec{k}_{3}\to 0
(and
p\to\pm\sqrt{s-m^{2}}
), and hence causes the IR divergence in the total cross section. When
p=0
it vanishes.

Consider now a general process involving incoming and outgoing BPs. Adding one soft NGB will cause an IR divergence only via a
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
insertion in one of the BP’s external legs. If the BP is incoming with momentum
(p,k_{1})
and the NGB has momentum
k_{2}
, the diagram is modified as follows:

{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.32pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 12.41 8.3 L 36.37 8.3}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{24.66742pt}{6.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 34.13 8.3)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 L 0.71 1.99 L 2.35 0 L 0.71 -1.99 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{14.21382pt}{10.83301pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 19.67 14.99)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{}
\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray={3.0pt,3.0pt},stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.5pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.95 0 L 50.23 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}}\to\frac{i\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}(-k_{2}\cdot((k_{1}+k_{2})+k_{1})ip)\frac{i}{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}=\frac{i\beta p}{\sqrt{8m}}.

(F.5)

Hence, for each external BP with
x
-momentum
p
, the diagram is multiplied by
f(p)\equiv\frac{i\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}p
to get the IR divergence, and all in all the diagram is multiplied by the factor:
\sum_{i}f(p_{i})=\frac{i\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}\sum_{i}p_{i}
, where incoming momenta are counted with a minus sign. Thus, the IR divergence vanishes if the
x
-momentum is conserved
\sum_{i}p_{i}=0
.

When two or more soft NGBs are added, this factor exponentiates, maintaining the elimination of the IR divergence when the
x
-momentum is conserved. We will show this for two additional NGBs and the generalization to three or more is simple. Adding the two NGBs together with
\mathcal{V}_{2A}
does not give a finite contribution when the NGBs become soft. Taking an outgoing BP for example gives

\lxSVG@closescope }\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray={3.0pt,3.0pt},stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.5pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.24 1.92 L 50.23 43.05}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}}\to\\
&\frac{i\beta^{2}}{\sqrt{4m}}(-(k_{2}\cdot k_{3})p^{2})\frac{i}{(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}=\frac{\beta^{2}p^{2}}{8m}\frac{k_{2}\cdot k_{3}}{k_{1}\cdot k_{2}+k_{1}\cdot k_{3}+k_{2}\cdot k_{3}}.\end{split}

(F.6)

This factor vanishes when
k_{2}
or
k_{3}
goes to zero (
k_{1}
is finite). So only NGBs added via
\mathcal{V}_{3A}
lead to IR divergences. They can be on two different legs or on the same leg. In the first case, the diagram is modified by
2f(p_{i})f(p_{j})
; the factor of
2
comes from the two possibilities of inserting the two NGBs (they have different momenta). In the second case, there are also two possibilities regarding the order of the two NGBs. Taking an outgoing BP for example:
