}}+(k_{2}\leftrightarrow k_{3})\to\\
&\left(\frac{i\beta}{\sqrt{8m}}ip\right)^{2}\frac{i(-k_{3}\cdot(2k_{1}+2k_{2}+k_{3}))}{(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{i(-k_{2}\cdot(2k_{1}+k_{2}))}{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}-p^{2}-m^{2}}+(k_{2}\leftrightarrow k_{3})\\
=&-\frac{\beta^{2}p^{2}}{8m}\frac{k_{3}\cdot(k_{1}+k_{2})}{k_{1}\cdot k_{2}+k_{1}\cdot k_{3}+k_{2}\cdot k_{3}}+(k_{2}\leftrightarrow k_{3})=-\frac{\beta^{2}}{8m}\left(1+\frac{k_{2}\cdot k_{3}}{k_{1}\cdot k_{2}+k_{1}\cdot k_{3}+k_{2}\cdot k_{3}}\right).\end{split}

(F.7)

The first term is
f(p)^{2}
, and the second term is the same as encountered with the
\mathcal{V}_{2A}
vertex, vanishing in the soft limit (they also cancel each other, so the total contribution is exactly
f(p)^{2}
). Therefore, the total contribution is:

\sum_{i\neq j}2f(p_{i})f(p_{j})+\sum_{i}f(p_{i})^{2}=\left(\sum_{i}f(p_{i})\right)^{2},

(F.8)

as we claimed.

Appendix G
Integrals and Sums

In this appendix, we derive some of the identities used in the paper.

G.1
Flux-Tube Profile

We demonstrate the cancellation of the divergence (
4.14
), at first nontrivial order (
O\left(\beta\right)
). There, we have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{x}\phi\right\rangle_{\beta}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{\beta^{0}}\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\frac{1}{2}\frac{\beta^{2}}{8m}\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon_{M}}p^{2}}_{E_{1}}

(G.1)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{{\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{\beta^{2}}}\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)}_{E_{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{{\rm\beta^{0}}}\intop dxe^{-ipx}{\left\langle\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(x,y,t\right)\right\rangle_{\beta}}}_{E_{3}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\intop dxe^{-ipx}{\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(x,y,t\right)\right\rangle_{\beta;{\rm con}}}}_{E_{4}}.

E_{2}
vanishes, because
\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{\beta^{2}}
vanishes. The only viable diagram comes from
\mathcal{V}_{3}
, but this vanishes by symmetry considerations in the
\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}
loop.
E_{4}
vanishes because there are no viable diagrams. We have:

\displaystyle E_{1}=\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{\beta^{0}}\beta A_{1},\ A_{1}\equiv\frac{\pi}{8m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2}\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon_{M}}.

(G.2)

We need to examine
E_{3}
also. Consider:

\displaystyle E_{3}=-\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{\beta^{0}}A_{2},\ A_{2}\equiv-\intop dxe^{-ipx}\left\langle\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(x,y,t\right)\right\rangle_{\beta}/\beta.

(G.3)

This gets a contribution from the vertex
V_{3BP}
, from the cubic vertex
\mathcal{V}_{1}
, from the tadpole counterterms:

-\beta\delta m_{2}^{2}\left(2\tanh(mx)\sech(mx)\right)\delta\phi_{\rm bulk},

(G.4)

and from the Jacobian:

\frac{\beta}{4}\delta^{2}\left(0\right)\sech(mx)\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}.

(G.5)

The contribution from the
\mathcal{V}_{1}

vertex cancels formally with the Jacobian counterterm because:

-\frac{\beta}{4}\left\langle\left(\partial x_{0}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{\beta^{0}}=-\frac{\beta}{4}\intop\frac{d^{2}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}\frac{iq_{a}\left(-iq^{a}\right)}{p^{2}}=-\frac{\beta}{4}\delta^{2}\left(0\right),

(G.6)

which is just right to be canceled by the counterterm. Notice that
the cancellation is complete, even when we compactify the
y
direction,
since the counterterm changes too, as it arises from the Jacobian. This is in contrast with ordinary counterterms in the Lagrangian, whose coefficients cannot depend on non-local information such as
L_{y}
. The remaining contributions give:

\displaystyle-\left\langle\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(x,\sigma\right)\right\rangle_{\beta}/\beta

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial_{x}\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\left(x,y,t\right)\left(\frac{2m^{2}}{3!}\tanh(mx)\sech(mx)\left(\delta\phi_{\rm bulk}\right)^{3}+{\rm c.t.}\right)\right\rangle_{\beta^{0}}

(G.7)

\displaystyle=\intop d{x^{\prime}}\left(m^{2}G_{{\rm bulk}}\left({x^{\prime}},0;{x^{\prime}},0\right)-2\delta m_{2}^{2}\right)\sinh(m{x'})\sech^{2}(m{x^{\prime}})\partial_{x}G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;{x^{\prime}}\right),

with:

\displaystyle G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;{x^{\prime}}\right)\equiv\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}e^{ip\left(x-{x^{\prime}}\right)}\frac{\left(ip+m\tanh\left(m{x^{\prime}}\right)\right)\left(-ip+m\tanh(mx)\right)}{p^{2}+m^{2}}.

(G.8)

G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}(x,x^{\prime})
appears after integrating the propagator
G_{{\rm bulk}}\left(x,\sigma;x^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\right)
over the vertex’s position in the parallel directions
\sigma^{\prime}
. The BP loop
G_{{\rm bulk}}\left({x^{\prime}},0;{x^{\prime}},0\right)
is placed at
\sigma=0
by translation symmetry. It is natural to separate the loop into a “bulk part” and a “string part”:

\displaystyle\left(m^{2}G_{{\rm bulk}}\left({x^{\prime}},0;{x^{\prime}},0\right)-2\delta m_{2}^{2}\right)

(G.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{2}\underbrace{\left(\frac{\mu^{-\epsilon}}{L_{y}}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop\frac{d^{2+\epsilon}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2+\epsilon}}\frac{1}{p^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+m^{2}}-2\frac{\delta m_{2}^{2}}{m^{2}}\right)}_{\equiv I_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left(-\frac{\mu^{-\epsilon}}{L_{y}}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop\frac{d^{1+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{1}{q^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\right)}_{\equiv I_{2}}\frac{1}{2}m^{3}{\sech}^{2}\left(m{x^{\prime}}\right).

Note that in
I_{1}
,
p
denotes the momenta in all
2+\epsilon
noncompact directions (including
x)
. Thus:

\displaystyle A_{2}=

\displaystyle I_{1}B_{1}+I_{2}B_{2},

(G.10)

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=m^{2}\intop dxe^{-ipx}\intop d{x^{\prime}}\sinh(m{x'})\sech^{2}(m{x^{\prime}})\partial_{x}G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;{x^{\prime}}\right)

(G.11)

\displaystyle=m\intop dxe^{-ipx}\intop d{x^{\prime}}\sech(m{x'})\partial_{x}\partial_{{x^{\prime}}}G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;{x^{\prime}}\right),

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}m^{3}\intop dxe^{-ipx}\intop d{x^{\prime}}\sinh(m{x'})\sech^{4}(m{x^{\prime}})\partial_{x}G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;{x^{\prime}}\right)

(G.12)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}m^{2}\intop dxe^{-ipx}\intop d{x^{\prime}}\sech^{3}(m{x^{\prime}})\partial_{x}\partial_{{x^{\prime}}}G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;{x^{\prime}}\right).

The
B
’s contain the
p
dependence, while the
I
’s act as
coefficients. The term
I_{1}B_{1}
represents the “pure bulk”
contribution, and is finite, thanks to the counterterm. Our main interest
is in
I_{2}B_{2}
, which is divergent, and needs to cancel the divergence
in
A_{1}
. The next subsections are devoted to the calculation of these four quantities. The results, (
G.17
), (
G.22
), (
G.28
) and (
G.32
) are:

\begin{split}B_{1}&=\frac{\pi}{4m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\pi p\tanh\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right),\\
I_{1}&=-\frac{\log\left(1-e^{-mL_{y}}\right)}{2\pi L_{y}},\\
B_{2}&=\frac{\pi}{8m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2},\\
I_{2}&=\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon_{M}}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m}{2M}\right)+O\left(L_{y}^{-1}\right).\end{split}

(G.13)

We see that
B_{2}
has the right
p
dependence to cancel the
divergence in
A_{1}
, and
I_{2}
has the right coefficient:

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{x}\phi\right\rangle_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{\beta^{0}}\frac{\pi}{8m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2}\left(-\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon_{M}}+I_{2}\right)+\cdots

(G.14)

\displaystyle=\left\langle:e^{-ipx_{0}\left(y,t\right)}:_{M}\right\rangle_{\beta^{0}}\frac{\pi}{8m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m}{2M}\right)+O\left(L_{y}^{-1}\right)\right)+\cdots.

G.1.1
I_{1}

\displaystyle I_{1}=\frac{\mu^{-\epsilon}}{L_{y}}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop\frac{d^{2+\epsilon}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2+\epsilon}}\frac{1}{p^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+m^{2}}-2\frac{\delta m_{2}^{2}}{m^{2}}.

(G.15)

Conveniently, the counterterm just gives the non-compact limit of
the integral:

\displaystyle 2\frac{\delta m_{2}^{2}}{m^{2}}=\mu^{-\epsilon}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop\frac{d^{3+\epsilon}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3+\epsilon}}\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}},

(G.16)

so after summing over
n_{y}
(and integrating over
p_{y}
for the counterterm)
we can combine the two terms under the same integral sign and remove
the regulator. Then we change to
E=\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}}
:

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{d^{2+\epsilon}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2+\epsilon}}\frac{\coth\left(\frac{1}{2}L_{y}\sqrt{m^{2}+p^{2}}\right)}{2\sqrt{m^{2}+p^{2}}}-2\frac{\delta m_{2}^{2}}{m^{2}}

(G.17)

\displaystyle=\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{d^{2+\epsilon}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2+\epsilon}}\frac{\coth\left(\frac{1}{2}L_{y}\sqrt{m^{2}+p^{2}}\right)-1}{2\sqrt{m^{2}+p^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\intop\frac{d^{2}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}\frac{\coth\left(\frac{1}{2}L_{y}\sqrt{m^{2}+p^{2}}\right)-1}{2\sqrt{m^{2}+p^{2}}}+O\left(\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\intop_{m}^{\infty}dE\left(\coth\left(\frac{1}{2}L_{y}E\right)-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{4\pi}-\frac{\log\left(2\sinh\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2}\right)\right)}{2\pi L_{y}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\log\left(1-e^{-mL_{y}}\right)}{2\pi L_{y}}.

G.1.2
I_{2}

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle-\intop\frac{d^{2}qdp}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3}}\frac{1}{q^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}

(G.18)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle-\mu^{-\epsilon}\frac{1}{L_{y}}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop\frac{d^{1+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{1}{q^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi}{L_{y}}n_{y}\right)^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}.

The non-compact case:

\displaystyle I_{2;\text{non-compact}}=

\displaystyle-\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{d^{2+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2+\epsilon}}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{1}{q^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}

(G.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{m2^{-\epsilon-1}\pi^{-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\csc\left(\frac{\pi\epsilon}{2}\right)\left(k^{2}+m^{2}\right)^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}-1}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\epsilon+2}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}2^{-\epsilon-1}\mu^{-\epsilon}\pi^{-\frac{\epsilon}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}m^{\epsilon}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}+\frac{-2\log(\frac{\mu}{m})+\gamma-\log(\pi)}{4\pi}.

To compute the compact case, we add and subtract the non-compact:

\displaystyle I_{2}=I_{2;\text{non-compact}}+\left(I_{2}-I_{2;\text{non-compact}}\right).

(G.20)

To evaluate the difference, we can carry out first the
n_{y}
sum (and
q_{y}
integral for the non-compact subtraction) to combine the two under the same integral sign, and then remove the regulator. It is then simple to change to polar coordinates in the
q_{t}-p
plane and integrate over the angle
\theta
. The final integral over
r=\sqrt{p^{2}+q_{t}^{2}}
cannot be evaluated in closed form, but can be cleaned up a bit by changing variables to
E=\sqrt{r^{2}+m^{2}}
and then
x=E/m-1\in\left(0,\infty\right)
:

\displaystyle I_{2}-I_{2;\text{non-compact}}

(G.21)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\mu^{-\epsilon}\frac{1}{L_{y}}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop\frac{d^{1+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{1}{q^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi}{L_{y}}n_{y}\right)^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}}\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}-{\rm reg}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{d^{1+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{m\coth\left(\frac{1}{2}L_{y}\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}\right)}{\left(p^{2}+m^{2}\right)\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}}-{\rm reg}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\intop\frac{dq}{2\pi}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{m\left(\coth\left(\frac{1}{2}L_{y}\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}\right)-1\right)}{\left(p^{2}+m^{2}\right)\sqrt{p^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}}+O\left(\epsilon\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\intop_{0}^{\infty}dx\frac{1}{\pi\left(e^{mL_{y}+x}-1\right)\left(mL_{y}+x\right)}+O\left(\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\text{Ei}\left(-mnL_{y}\right)+O\left(\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-mnL_{y}}\left(-\frac{1}{mnL_{y}}+O\left(L_{y}^{-2}\right)\right)+O\left(\epsilon\right).

In summary:

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}+\frac{-2\log(\frac{\mu}{m})+\gamma-\log(\pi)}{4\pi}-\intop_{0}^{\infty}dx\frac{1}{\pi\left(e^{mL_{y}+x}-1\right)\left(mL_{y}+x\right)}+O\left(\epsilon\right)

(G.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}+\frac{-2\log(\frac{\mu}{m})+\gamma-\log(\pi)}{4\pi}+\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-mnL_{y}}\left(-\frac{1}{mnL_{y}}+O\left(L_{y}^{-2}\right)\right)+O\left(\epsilon\right).

G.1.3
B_{1}

Recall that:

\displaystyle G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;x_{2}\right)=\intop\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}}e^{ip^{\prime}\left(x-x_{2}\right)}\frac{\left(ip^{\prime}+m\tanh\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)\left(-ip^{\prime}+m\tanh(mx)\right)}{p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}}.

(G.23)

Let us compute
B_{1}
:

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=m\intop dxe^{-ipx}\intop dx_{2}\sech(mx_2)\partial_{x}\partial_{x_{2}}G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;x_{2}\right).

(G.24)

First note:

\displaystyle\intop dx_{2}\sech\left(mx_{2}\right)\partial_{x_{2}}\left(e^{p^{\prime}x_{2}}\left(ip^{\prime}+m\tanh\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)\right)

(G.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\intop dx_{2}e^{-ip^{\prime}x_{2}}\sech\left(mx_{2}\right)\left(\cancel{-ip^{\prime}\left(ip^{\prime}+m\tanh\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)}+m^{2}\sech^{2}\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{2}\intop dx_{2}e^{-ip^{\prime}x_{2}}\sech^{3}\left(mx_{2}\right)\,\text{(by orthogonality)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2m}\left(m^{2}+p^{\prime 2}\right)\sech\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right).

We will need another side-integral:

\displaystyle\intop dx\intop\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)e^{i\left(p^{\prime}-p\right)x}\frac{\tanh(mx)}{p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}}=\frac{1}{\pi m}\frac{-i\left(\pi\left(m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)-2mp\right)\pi\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)}{2m\left(p^{2}+m^{2}\right)}.

(G.26)

Putting it all together:

\displaystyle\intop dxdx_{2}e^{-ipx}\sech\left(mx_{2}\right)\partial_{x}\partial_{x_{2}}G_{{\rm\perp}}\left(x,x_{2}\right)

(G.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\intop dxdx_{2}e^{-ipx}\sech\left(mx_{2}\right)\partial_{x}\partial_{x_{2}}\intop\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\left(e^{ip^{\prime}\left(x-x_{2}\right)}\frac{\left(ip^{\prime}+m\tanh\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)\left(-ip^{\prime}+m\tanh(mx)\right)}{\left(p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2m}ip\intop dx\intop\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)e^{i\left(p^{\prime}-p\right)x}\frac{\left(-ip^{\prime}+m\tanh(mx)\right)}{p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\frac{p^{2}}{p^{2}+m^{2}}+\frac{\pi}{2}ip\intop dx\intop\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)e^{i\left(p^{\prime}-p\right)x}\frac{\tanh(mx)}{p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)\frac{p^{2}}{p^{2}+m^{2}}+\frac{p}{2m}\frac{\left(\pi\left(m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)-2mp\right)\pi\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)}{2m\left(p^{2}+m^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\pi}{2m}\right)^{2}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p\tanh\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right).

So we find:

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{2}}{4m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p\tanh\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right).

(G.28)

G.1.4
B_{2}

\displaystyle B_{2}=\frac{1}{6}m^{2}\intop dxe^{-ipx}\intop dx_{2}\sech^{3}(mx_{2})\partial_{x}\partial_{x_{2}}G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}\left(x;x_{2}\right).

(G.29)

First, note that:

\displaystyle\intop dx_{2}\sech^{3}\left(mx_{2}\right)\partial_{x_{2}}\left(e^{-ip^{\prime}x_{2}}\left(ip^{\prime}+m\tanh\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)\right)

(G.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\intop dx_{2}e^{-ip^{\prime}x_{2}}\sech^{3}\left(mx_{2}\right)\left(-ip^{\prime}\left(ip^{\prime}+m\tanh\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)+m^{2}\sech^{2}\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\intop dx_{2}e^{-ip^{\prime}x_{2}}\left(p^{\prime 2}\sech^{3}\left(mx_{2}\right)-ip^{\prime}m\sech^{4}\left(mx_{2}\right)\sinh\left(mx_{2}\right)+m^{2}\sech^{5}\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\intop dx_{2}e^{-ip^{\prime}x_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{3}p^{\prime 2}\sech^{3}\left(mx_{2}\right)+m^{2}\sech^{5}\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi\left(p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}\right){}^{2}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)}{8m^{3}}.

The factor of
\left(p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}\right)^{2}
is just right to cancel the denominator in
G_{{\rm bulk};\perp}
. We use this to carry out the
x_{2}
integral in:

\displaystyle\intop dxdx_{2}e^{-ipx}\sech^{3}\left(mx_{2}\right)\partial_{x}\partial_{x_{2}}G_{{\rm\perp}}\left(x,x_{2}\right)

(G.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\intop dxdx_{2}\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\sech^{3}\left(mx_{2}\right)e^{-ipx}\partial_{x}\partial_{x_{2}}\left(e^{ip^{\prime}\left(x-x_{2}\right)}\frac{\left(ip^{\prime}+m\tanh\left(mx_{2}\right)\right)\left(-ip^{\prime}+m\tanh(mx)\right)}{\left(p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi}{8m^{3}}\intop dx\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)ipe^{i\left(p^{\prime}-p\right)x}\left(-ip-ip^{\prime}+\left(ip+m\tanh(mx)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi}{4m^{3}}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2}+\frac{3\pi}{8m^{3}}ip\intop dx\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)e^{i\left(p^{\prime}-p\right)x}\left(ip+m\tanh(mx)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi}{4m^{3}}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2}+\frac{3}{8m^{2}}ip\cancel{\intop dx\sech(mx)e^{-ipx}\left(ip+m\tanh(mx)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi}{4m^{3}}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2}\,

where in the before-last line we used orthogonality of the eigenfunctions.
In summary, we find:

\displaystyle B_{2}=\frac{\pi}{8m}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)p^{2}.

(G.32)

G.2
Ground-State Energy

G.2.1
PI Framework

For
E_{{\rm string}}
we evaluate the sum-integral:

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n_{y}}\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{d^{1+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}}\left(\log\left(q^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}\right)-\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\log\left(q^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}\right)\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\right).

(G.33)

Before regularization and compactification, this is (
5.5
):

\frac{1}{2}L_{y}\intop\frac{d^{2}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}\left(\log\left(q^{2}\right)-\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\log\left(q^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}\right)\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\right).

(G.34)

We can carry out the integral over the pseudo-momentum

p
, this commutes with dim-regularization and
y
-compactification:

\displaystyle\dots=

\displaystyle-L_{y}\intop\frac{d^{2}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}{\rm arctanh}\left(\frac{m}{\sqrt{m^{2}+q^{2}}}\right).

(G.35)

Now, we regulate, compactify, and spherically integrate in the noncompact directions:

\displaystyle-L_{y}\intop\frac{d^{2}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}{\rm arctanh}\left(\frac{m}{\sqrt{m^{2}+q^{2}}}\right)

(G.36)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle-\sum_{n_{y}}\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{d^{1+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}}{\rm arctanh}\left(\frac{m}{\sqrt{m^{2}+q^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\mu^{-\epsilon}\pi^{\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}\Gamma\left(\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}\right)}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop dqq^{\epsilon}{\rm arctanh}\left(\frac{m}{\sqrt{m^{2}+q^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}}}\right).

Integration by parts gives:

\displaystyle\dots=

\displaystyle-m\frac{2\mu^{-\epsilon}\pi^{\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}\Gamma\left(\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}\right)\left(1+\epsilon\right)}\sum_{n_{y}}\intop dqq^{2+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\left(\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+q^{2}\right)\sqrt{\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}}.

(G.37)

Note that the prefactor outside the sum-integral is finite at
\epsilon\to 0
.
By strategic addition and subtraction of a helper term, we separate
the expression into a finite-but-complicated part (for which we remove
the regulator) and a divergent-but-simple part, and evaluate the
q
-integral:

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{y}}\intop dqq^{2+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\left(\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+q^{2}\right)\sqrt{\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}}

(G.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{y}}\intop dqq^{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}}\left(\frac{1}{\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+q^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{y}}\intop dqq^{2+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\left(\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{L_{y}}\right)^{2}+m^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{y}}\left(1-\frac{2\pi\left|n_{y}\right|}{mL_{y}}\arctan\left(\left|\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi n_{y}}\right|\right)\right)+\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\epsilon+3}{2}\right)m^{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\sum_{n_{y}}\left(\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{mL_{y}}\right)^{2}+1\right)^{\epsilon/2}.

First, let us evaluate the divergent sum. Writing
a\equiv\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi}
, we can factor
\left(\frac{n_{y}}{a}\right)^{2}+1=a^{-2}\left(n_{y}+ia\right)\left(n_{y}-ia\right)
and combine the two linear polynomials using the Feynman trick:

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\sum_{n_{y}}\left(1+\left(\frac{n_{y}}{a}\right)^{2}\right)^{\epsilon/2}

(G.39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+2\frac{\Gamma\left(-\epsilon\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)}a^{-\epsilon}\intop_{0}^{1}dxx^{-1-\epsilon/2}\left(1-x\right)^{-1-\epsilon/2}\sum_{n_{y}>0}\left(ia\left(2x-1\right)+n_{y}\right)^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+2\frac{\Gamma\left(-\epsilon\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)}a^{-\epsilon}\intop_{0}^{1}dxx^{-1-\epsilon/2}\left(1-x\right)^{-1-\epsilon/2}\zeta(-\epsilon,1+ia\left(2x-1\right))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\frac{\Gamma\left(-\epsilon\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)}a^{-\epsilon}\intop_{0}^{1}dxx^{-1-\epsilon/2}\left(1-x\right)^{-1-\epsilon/2}\left(\zeta(-\epsilon,1+ia\left(2x-1\right))+{\rm c.c.}\right),

where
\zeta\left(z,w\right)
is the Hurwitz zeta function, and on
the last we line used the reflection
x\to 1-x
to make the integrand
manifestly real. We again separate into finite-but-complicated and
divergent-but-simple, this time by adding and subtracting the values
where
\zeta
is evaluated at the endpoint
x=1
, and remove
the regulator for the finite part:

\displaystyle\dots=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+a^{-\epsilon}\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\left(\zeta(-\epsilon,1+ia)+{\rm c.c.}\right)

(G.40)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\intop_{0}^{1}dxx^{-1}\left(1-x\right)^{-1}\left(\zeta(0,1+ia\left(2x-1\right))-\zeta(0,1+ia)+{\rm c.c.}\right).

It turns out that at
\epsilon=0
, the real part of
\zeta(0,1+ia\left(2x-1\right))

is
x
-independent, and so the entire finite part cancels exactly.
We also find that the divergences in the putative divergent part cancel,
leaving:

\displaystyle\dots=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\left(1-\epsilon\log\left(a\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)\right)\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\left(-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon\left(\partial_{\epsilon}\zeta(-\epsilon,1+ia)\Big|_{\epsilon=0}+{\rm c.c.}\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)\right)

(G.41)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\intop_{0}^{1}dxx^{-1}\left(1-x\right)^{-1}\left(-1-\left(-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(-\log\left(a\right)-\left(\partial_{\epsilon}\zeta(-\epsilon,1+ia)\Big|_{\epsilon=0}+{\rm c.c.}\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(-\log\left(a\right)+\left(\log\left(\Gamma\left(1+ia\right)\Gamma\left(1-ia\right)\right)-\log\left(2\pi\right)\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(-\log\left(a\right)+\log\left(\pi a{\rm csch}\left(\pi a\right)\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)-\log\left(\sinh\left(\pi a\right)\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{1}\right)\right).

Thus:

\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\epsilon+3}{2}\right)m^{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Gamma\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\sum_{n_{y}}\left(\left(\frac{2\pi n_{y}}{mL_{y}}\right)^{2}+1\right)^{\epsilon/2}=2\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)-\log\left(\sinh\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2}\right)\right)\right).

(G.42)

For the finite part in (
G.38
), we evaluate it by taking an
a
-derivative and integrating back:

\displaystyle\dots

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{y}-\infty}^{\infty}\left(1-\frac{2\pi\left|n_{y}\right|}{mL_{y}}\arctan\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi\left|n_{y}\right|}\right)\right)

(G.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{mL_{y}}\sum_{n_{y}-\infty}^{\infty}\intop_{0}^{\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi}}da\partial_{a}\left(a-\left|n_{y}\right|\arctan\left(\frac{a}{\left|n_{y}\right|}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{mL_{y}}\sum_{n_{y}-\infty}^{\infty}\intop_{0}^{\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi}}da\frac{a^{2}}{a^{2}+n_{y}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{mL_{y}}\intop_{0}^{\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi}}da\pi a\coth\left(\pi a\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{mL_{y}}\frac{1}{6}\left(\frac{3}{2}m^{2}L_{y}^{2}-6\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right)+6mL_{y}\log\left(1-e^{-mL_{y}}\right)+\pi^{2}\right).

Putting together the finite and (putative-)divergent parts back into (
G.38
):

\displaystyle\dots=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{mL_{y}}\frac{1}{6}\left(\frac{3}{2}m^{2}L_{y}^{2}-6\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right)+6mL_{y}\log\left(1-e^{-mL_{y}}\right)+\pi^{2}\right)

(G.44)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}{\sqrt{\pi}}2\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)-\log\left(\sinh\left(\pi\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi}\right)\right)\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{mL_{y}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6mL_{y}}-\frac{1}{mL_{y}}\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right)+O\left(\epsilon\right).

Remembering the prefactor from (
G.37
), we get:

\displaystyle-m\frac{2\mu^{-\epsilon}\pi^{\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{1+\epsilon}\Gamma\left(\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}\right)\left(1+\epsilon\right)}\left(-\frac{mL_{y}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6mL_{y}}-\frac{1}{mL_{y}}\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{1}\right)\right)

(G.45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}L_{y}}{4\pi}-\frac{\pi}{6L_{y}}+\frac{1}{\pi L_{y}}\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{1}\right),

which is the final answer. In summary (suppressing regularization and compactification):

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}L_{y}\intop\frac{d^{2}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}\left(\log\left(q^{2}\right)-\intop\frac{dp}{2\pi}\log\left(q^{2}+p^{2}+m^{2}\right)\frac{2m}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\right)

(G.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}L_{y}}{4\pi}-\frac{\pi}{6L_{y}}+\frac{1}{\pi L_{y}}\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right).

There is one further contribution to
\delta E_{\rm string}
at this order, stemming from the on-shell evaluation of the cosine-potential counterterm:

\frac{4\delta m_{2}^{2}}{m}L_{y}.

(G.47)

The value of
\delta m_{2}^{2}
can be obtained from the renormalization condition, setting the true mass of the photon to
m
. It is tuned to cancel the single 1-loop contribution to the photon propagator (in the vacuum, not the 1-string background). We find:

\displaystyle\delta m_{2}^{2}=\frac{m^{2}}{2}\mu^{-\epsilon}\intop\frac{d^{3+\epsilon}q}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3+\epsilon}}\frac{1}{q^{2}+m^{2}}=-\frac{m^{3}}{8\pi}.

(G.48)

Combining this with (
G.46
), we find:

\displaystyle\delta E_{\rm string}=

\displaystyle-\frac{m^{2}L_{y}}{4\pi}-\frac{\pi}{6L_{y}}+\frac{1}{\pi L_{y}}\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right).

(G.49)

G.2.2
Canonical Framework

We need to evaluate the sum (
5.22
):

\begin{split}\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-s|q_{l}|}&\Bigg(\frac{q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m^{2}-q_{l}^{2}}{2mq_{l}}\right)-q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m}{q_{l}}\right)}{\pi}+\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)\\
&+\frac{\pi\sqrt{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}-2m}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\left(|q_{l}|-\sqrt{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}\right)\Bigg)=\\
\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-s|q_{l}|}&\left(\frac{2q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m^{2}-q_{l}^{2}}{2mq_{l}}\right)-2q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m}{q_{l}}\right)+\pi|q_{l}|-2m}{2\pi}+\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)\right).\end{split}

(G.50)

The first term in the parenthesis is finite; it goes as
\frac{1}{l^{2}}
as
l\to\infty
. Therefore, we can evaluate it with
s=0
. In order to evaluate the sum, we take a derivative with respect to
m
, evaluate the sum, and then integrate it again. The integration constant can be fixed by requiring the sum to vanish for
m=0
, for which the numerator vanishes
2q_{l}\left(-\frac{\pi}{2\text{sign}(q_{l})}\right)-0+\pi|q_{l}|-0=0
. Hence:

\begin{split}&\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m^{2}-q_{l}^{2}}{2mq_{l}}\right)-2q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m}{q_{l}}\right)+\pi|q_{l}|-2m}{2\pi}\right)=\\
-\frac{m}{\pi}+2&\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m^{2}-q_{l}^{2}}{2mq_{l}}\right)-2q_{l}\arctan\left(\frac{m}{q_{l}}\right)+\pi|q_{l}|-2m}{2\pi}\right)=\\
-\frac{m}{\pi}+2&\int_{0}^{m}dm^{\prime}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\left(-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{m^{\prime 2}}{m^{\prime 2}+q_{l}^{2}}\right)=-\frac{m}{\pi}+2\int_{0}^{m}dm^{\prime}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2-L_{y}m^{\prime}\coth\left(\frac{L_{y}m^{\prime}}{2}\right)}{4\pi}\right)=\\
&-\frac{m^{2}}{4\pi}L_{y}-\frac{\pi}{6L_{y}}-\frac{m\log\left(1-e^{-mL_{y}}\right)}{\pi}+\frac{\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right)}{\pi L_{y}}.\end{split}

(G.51)

The second term in (
G.50
) diverges and will be canceled with the counterterm divergence. We need:

\begin{split}\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-s|q_{l}|}\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)=\frac{m}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{m}{2\Lambda}\right)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}e^{-s|q_{l}|}\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right).\end{split}

(G.52)

For
l\neq 0
, it is convenient to split it into a convergent part and a divergent part:

\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)=\frac{m}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\pi}{\Lambda L_{y}}\right)+\frac{m}{\pi}\log(|l|)+\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(1+\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi l}\right)^{2}\right).

(G.53)

The sum over the first two terms is divergent and requires the regulator
s
, while the third one does not. The sum of the first term is trivial:

2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\pi}{\Lambda L_{y}}\right)e^{-\frac{2\pi sl}{L_{y}}}=\frac{2m}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\pi}{\Lambda L_{y}}\right)\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{L_{y}}{2\pi s}+O(s)\right).

(G.54)

For the second term we use
\log(l)=\frac{dl^{\epsilon}}{d\epsilon}\big|_{\epsilon=0}
, and then:

\begin{split}2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{\pi}l^{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{2\pi sl}{L_{y}}}&=\frac{2m}{\pi}\text{Li}_{-\epsilon}\left(e^{-\frac{2\pi s}{L_{y}}}\right)\Rightarrow\\
2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{\pi}\log(l)e^{-\frac{2\pi sl}{L_{y}}}&=\frac{2m\log(2\pi)}{L_{y}}-\frac{2m}{\pi s}\left(\log\left(\frac{2\pi s}{L_{y}}\right)+\gamma\right)+O(s).\end{split}

(G.55)

For the third term, we set
s=0
, differentiate by
m
, sum, and integrate. It should vanish for
m=0
. Hence:

\begin{split}2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(1+\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2\pi l}\right)^{2}\right)=\frac{m}{\pi}\int_{0}^{m}dm^{\prime}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{2L_{y}^{2}m^{\prime}}{L_{y}^{2}m^{\prime 2}+4\pi^{2}l^{2}}=\\
\frac{m}{\pi}\int_{0}^{m}dm^{\prime}\frac{-2+m^{\prime}L_{y}\coth\left(\frac{m^{\prime}L_{y}}{2}\right)}{2m}=\frac{m}{\pi}\left(\log\left(\sinh\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2}\right)\right)-\log\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2}\right)\right).\end{split}

(G.56)

Adding (
G.54
), (
G.55
), (
G.56
) together, one finds:

\begin{split}\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-s|q_{l}|}\frac{m}{2\pi}\log\left(\frac{m^{2}+q_{l}^{2}}{4\Lambda^{2}}\right)=\frac{m}{\pi}\log\left(2\sinh\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2}\right)\right)-\frac{mL_{y}}{\pi^{2}s}(\log(2\La s)+\gamma)+O(s).\end{split}

(G.57)

With the counterterm (
5.11
), the correction (
5.22
) is:

\begin{split}\delta E_{\rm string}=&-\frac{m^{2}}{4\pi}L_{y}-\frac{\pi}{6L_{y}}-\frac{m\log\left(1-e^{-mL_{y}}\right)}{\pi}+\frac{\text{Li}_{2}\left(e^{-mL_{y}}\right)}{\pi L_{y}}\\
&+\frac{m}{\pi}\log\left(2\sinh\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2}\right)\right)-\frac{mL_{y}}{\pi^{2}s}(\log(2\La s)+\gamma)+\\
&+\frac{m^{2}\beta^{2}}{4}\left(\frac{1}{\pi^{2}s}(\gamma+\log(2\La s))-\frac{m}{2\pi}\right)\frac{4L_{y}}{m\beta^{2}},\end{split}

(G.58)

which is (
5.7
), using:

-\frac{m\log\left(1-e^{-mL_{y}}\right)}{\pi}+\frac{m}{\pi}\log\left(2\sinh\left(\frac{mL_{y}}{2}\right)\right)-\frac{m^{2}L_{y}}{2\pi}=0.

(G.59)

G.3
Scattering

Below, the integrals used for calculating scattering cross sections are evaluated. All are Wick-rotated to Euclidean variable
s_{L}\to-s_{E}
, where the propagator is changed:

\frac{1}{s_{L}-p^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\to-\frac{1}{s_{E}+p^{2}+m^{2}},

(G.60)

which will be our starting point. The answer is rotated back to Lorentzian variable by
s_{E}\to-s_{L}
. The factor
\sqrt{s_{E}+m^{2}}
appearing in the Euclidean result is analytically-continued to
(-i)^{\theta\left(s_{L}-m^{2}\right)}\sqrt{|s_{L}-m^{2}|}
. The sign of the square root for
s_{L}>m^{2}
can be dictated by the imaginary part (
6.7
) being positive. It can also be seen by the usual relation
E_{E}=-iE_{L}
. Going from Lorentzian energy to Euclidean energy amounts to the trajectory
E_{L}=Ee^{i\varphi}
for
0\leq\varphi\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
in the complex Lorentzian energy. For the complex Euclidean energy it corresponds to
E_{E}=-iEe^{i\varphi}
or
s_{E}=-se^{2i\varphi}
. The factor
\sqrt{s_{E}+m^{2}}
has a branch cut on the negative real
s_{E}
axis, starting from
s_{E}=-m^{2}
. For
s_{L}=-s_{E}
between
-m^{2}
and
0
, there is no ambiguity, and the positive square root remains. The region
s_{L}>m^{2}
is on the branch cut. Going back along
s_{E}=se^{-2i\varphi}
for
0\leq\varphi\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
amounts to taking the square root with negative imaginary part, which also dictates that
\sqrt{s_{E}+m^{2}}=-i\sqrt{s_{L}-m^{2}}
in this region.

G.3.1
I_{\rm 2NGB}

This integral is evaluated by Fourier-transforming to position space:

-\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}}{s+p^{2}+m^{2}}\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)^{2}=-\frac{m}{2\pi^{3}}\int dpdxdy\frac{p^{2}+1}{\frac{s}{m^{2}}+p^{2}+1}\sech(x)\sech(y)e^{ip(x+y)},

(G.61)

where we used the Fourier transform of
\sech
:

\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2}\right)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int dxe^{ipx}\sech(x).

(G.62)

The integral over
p
is now a simple Fourier transform:

\displaystyle\int dp\frac{e^{ipx}}{a^{2}+p^{2}}=\frac{\pi e^{-a|x|}}{a}.

(G.63)

Changing variables
x=z+w,y=z-w
, the integrals of
w
and
z
are:

\displaystyle\int dw\sech(w+z)\sech(z-w)

\displaystyle=2z\csch(z)\sech(z),

(G.64)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}dz\frac{ze^{-az}}{\sinh(2z)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\psi_{1}\left(\frac{a+2}{4}\right).

(G.65)

The evaluation of the integral in subsection
E.2
follows similar lines to the evaluation of
I_{\rm 2NGB}
. One needs a few more Fourier transforms:

\displaystyle\int dp\frac{p^{2}e^{ipx}}{a^{2}+p^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\pi ae^{-a|x|}+2\pi\delta(x),

(G.66)

\displaystyle\int dp\frac{p^{4}e^{ipx}}{a^{2}+p^{2}}

\displaystyle=\pi a^{3}e^{-a|x|}-2\pi a^{2}\delta(x)-2\pi\delta^{\prime\prime}(x),

(G.67)

\displaystyle\int dp\frac{p^{6}e^{ipx}}{a^{2}+p^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\pi a^{5}e^{-a|x|}+2\pi a^{4}\delta(x)+2\pi a^{2}\delta^{\prime\prime}(x)+2\pi\delta^{(4)}(x).

(G.68)

The integral over
w
gives the same answer, and the integral over
z
contains other integrals over
\delta
-function and its derivative, which are easy to evaluate.

G.3.2
I_{\rm 1NGB1BP}

First, we manipulate the fraction:

\begin{split}&-\int\frac{dp^{\prime}}{2\pi}{\csch}\left(\frac{\pi(p+p^{\prime})}{2m}\right){\sech}\left(\frac{\pi p^{\prime}}{2m}\right)\frac{p^{\prime 2}-p^{2}}{s+p^{\prime 2}+m^{2}}=\\
&-\frac{m}{\pi^{2}}\int dp\frac{1}{\cosh(p)\sinh(a+p)}+\frac{m(a^{2}+b^{2})}{\pi^{2}}\int dp\frac{1}{\cosh(p)\sinh(a+p)}\frac{1}{b^{2}+p^{2}},\end{split}

(G.69)

where
a\equiv\frac{\pi p}{2m},\ b\equiv\frac{\pi}{2}\sqrt{\frac{s}{m^{2}}+1}
. The first integral is solved by a change of variables
x=e^{2p}
:

\begin{split}\int dp\frac{1}{\cosh(p)\sinh(a+p)}=2a\sech(a).\end{split}

(G.70)

The second integral is more involved. First, we manipulate it:

\int dp\frac{1}{b^{2}+p^{2}}\frac{1}{\cosh(p)\sinh(a+p)}=4\sech(a)e^{2a}\int dy\frac{1}{e^{2a}-e^{-y}}\frac{1}{4b^{2}+y^{2}}-4\sech(a)\int dy\frac{1}{1+e^{-y}}\frac{1}{4b^{2}+y^{2}},

(G.71)

with
y=2p
. The last integral is solved by using
1=\frac{1}{1+e^{y}}+\frac{e^{y}}{1+e^{y}}
and changing
y\to-y
inside the integral. The result is:

\int dy\frac{1}{1+e^{-y}}\frac{1}{4b^{2}+y^{2}}=\frac{\pi}{4b}.

(G.72)

The integral remaining is transformed into a series upon the residue theorem. On the upper half of the complex plane, it has poles at
2ib,\{2\pi ik,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}\}
. Then:

\int dy\frac{1}{e^{2a}-e^{-y}}\frac{1}{4b^{2}+y^{2}}=-2\pi\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\pi ae^{-2a}k}{2\left(a^{2}+(b-\pi k)^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+(b+\pi k)^{2}\right)}+\frac{\cos(2b)-e^{2a}}{4b\left(-2e^{2a}\cos(2b)+e^{4a}+1\right)}\right).

(G.73)

The series can be summed up as follows:

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k}{\left(a^{2}+(b-\pi k)^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+(b+\pi k)^{2}\right)}=\frac{i}{8\pi^{2}ab}\left(-H_{\frac{ia-b}{\pi}}-H_{\frac{b-ia}{\pi}}+H_{\frac{b+ia}{\pi}}+H_{-\frac{b+ia}{\pi}}\right).

(G.74)

This result is simplified by using the identity:

H_{-z}=H_{z}-\frac{1}{z}+\pi\cot(\pi z).

(G.75)

G.3.3
I^{1}_{\rm 2BP}

For solving the integral:

\begin{split}-\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}&\frac{\sech\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}+p_{2}+p)}{2m}\right)\sech\left(\frac{\pi p}{2m}\right)}{s+p^{2}+m^{2}}\cdot\\
&\left(3m^{4}+(-p_{1}+p_{2}+p)(p_{1}-p_{2}+p)(p_{1}+p_{2}-p)(p_{1}+p_{2}+p)+2m^{2}(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}+p^{2})\right),\end{split}

(G.76)

we write the fraction as
\frac{a_{4}p^{4}+a_{2}p^{2}+a_{0}}{p^{2}+b^{2}}=a_{2}-a_{4}b^{2}+a_{4}p^{2}+\frac{a_{0}-a_{2}b^{2}+a_{4}b^{4}}{b^{2}+p^{2}}
, with
b
as before. The integral of the polynomial part times the two sech’s is known:

\displaystyle\int dp\sech(p)\sech(p+a)

\displaystyle=2a\csch(a),

(G.77)

\displaystyle\int dp\sech(p)\sech(p+a)p^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{a(4a^{2}+\pi^{2})}{6}\csch(a).

(G.78)

The remaining integral is:

\int dp\frac{\sech(p)\sech(p+a)}{b^{2}+p^{2}}=-4\csch(a)\int dy\frac{1}{4b^{2}+y^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{1+e^{-y}}-\frac{e^{2a}}{e^{2a}+e^{-y}}\right).

(G.79)

The first integral was solved in (
G.72
). The second one is similar to (
G.73
), but its poles are shifted by
i\pi
, and its sum could not be solved directly by Mathematica. However, it can be decomposed as follows:

\frac{1}{e^{2a}+e^{-y}}=\frac{2e^{2a}}{e^{4a}-e^{-2y}}-\frac{1}{e^{2a}-e^{-y}}.

(G.80)

These two integrals are the same as in (
G.73
).

G.3.4
I^{2}_{\rm 2BP}

This integral can be solved using (
G.73
). First, we decompose

\begin{split}-\int\frac{dp}{2\pi}&\frac{1}{t+p^{2}+m^{2}}\frac{(p_{1}^{2}-p^{2})(p_{2}^{2}-p^{2})}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\csch\left(\frac{\pi(p_{1}-p)}{2m}\right)\csch\left(\frac{\pi(p_{2}+p)}{2m}\right)=\\
&\frac{m}{\pi^{2}}\int dp\csch(p-a_1)\csch(a_2+p)\\
+&\frac{m}{\pi^{2}}\frac{1}{b^{2}-c^{2}}\int dp\csch(p-a_1)\csch(a_2+p)\left(\frac{\left(a_{1}^{2}+c^{2}\right)\left(a_{2}^{2}+c^{2}\right)}{c^{2}+p^{2}}-\frac{\left(a_{1}^{2}+b^{2}\right)\left(a_{2}^{2}+b^{2}\right)}{b^{2}+p^{2}}\right),\end{split}

(G.81)

where
a_{1,2}\equiv\frac{\pi p_{1,2}}{2m},\ c\equiv\frac{\pi}{2}
and
b
as before. The first integral gives:

\int dp\csch(p-a_1)\csch(a_2+p)=\int dp\csch(p)\csch(a_1+a_2+p)=-2(a_{1}+a_{2})\csch(a_1+a_2).

(G.82)

The second integral can be decomposed further:

\begin{split}&\int dp\frac{1}{p^{2}+c^{2}}\csch(p-a_1)\csch(p+a_2)=\\
&\frac{8e^{a_{1}-a_{2}}}{e^{2a_{1}}-e^{-2a_{2}}}\left(e^{-2a_{1}}\int dy\frac{1}{y^{2}+4c^{2}}\frac{1}{e^{-2a_{1}}-e^{-y}}-e^{2a_{2}}\int dy\frac{1}{y^{2}+4c^{2}}\frac{1}{e^{2a_{2}}-e^{-y}}\right).\end{split}

(G.83)

These integrals are again the same as in (
G.73
).
